Client with IP address ... is not allowed to access the server`, and the long-query XE session could not enumerate databases, even though the database itself was fully reachable from SSMS. **This is a regression of the [#857] single-database fallback, introduced by [#1506].** That fix correctly stopped reading 40615 ("client IP not allowed at the logical server") as a statement about a login's RIGHTS to read `master` - it is a reachability error, and misreading it had permanently wedged a different user whose public IP rotated daily. But it also dropped 40615 from the single-database FALLBACK path, on the stated premise that "falling back to a user database is futile because the same rule blocks that connection too." That premise is wrong: Azure SQL Database evaluates **database-level** IP firewall rules BEFORE server-level ones, and a client whose IP matches a database-level rule (`sp_set_database_firewall_rule`) is granted a connection to that database with **no** server-level rule permitting it - while `master`, where server-level rules live, still requires one. "Blocked at the server, allowed at the database" is therefore a real, supported configuration that Microsoft explicitly recommends ("use database-level IP firewall rules whenever possible"), and it is precisely the [#857] case. The two questions - *"does this login lack rights to master?"* and *"should database-scoped collection degrade to single-database?"* - are now separated in the shared `SqlErrorClassification`: the rights list is unchanged (40615 still correctly excluded, so every [#1506] invariant holds), and a new fallback list is that set plus 40615, which is what both SKUs' Azure database-enumeration catch sites actually ask. Acting on it is safe because [#1506]'s other fix survives untouched: the resulting verdict is a revocable throttle, not a latch - it expires after 15 minutes, is discarded when a server returns from an outage, and is skipped entirely when there is no target database to fall back to. A client genuinely firewalled out with no database-level rule therefore just fails its fallback attempt with the same 40615, logs an ordinary collector error, and recovers on the next successful connect. Pinned from both suites, independently: 40615 is still not a rights denial, AND it does trigger the fallback; the rights set remains a strict subset of the fallback set; and the disjointness invariant is now asserted against the broader fallback set (40613 stays transient-and-retryable, never a reason to degrade). Lite + Darling.
- **Darling: collectors no longer fail with `22021: invalid byte sequence for encoding "UTF8": 0x00`** ([#1614]) - SQL Server NVARCHAR allows embedded NUL characters and query text from `sys.dm_exec_sql_text` sometimes carries them, but Postgres `text` columns reject the byte, so one NUL-laden cached query failed the entire `query_stats` COPY batch every cycle (`DBCC FREEPROCCACHE` never helped because the app re-caches the same query). The Postgres row writer now strips NULs from every collected string - query text, plan XML, deadlock and blocked-process XML included - at the single COPY choke point.
- **Darling Web: a custom view no longer resets the picked time range / server / filters every 60 seconds** ([#1619]) - the background refresh that keeps the dashboard live rebuilt each view from its declared default, snapping any range/server/filter change back within a minute. A per-view scope memory now survives the re-render, so a picked scope sticks until you change it or hard-reload.
- **Darling: the Custom Views composer and analyze_*_plan no longer time out on large stores** ([#1620]) - the columns the composer filters/groups by (`procedure_stats.object_name`, `query_stats.query_hash`, `query_store_stats.query_hash`) and the single-row `analyze_*_plan` lookups (`sql_handle` / `query_id`+`plan_id`) had no supporting index, so on a large store they fell to a Seq Scan and hit the 15 s statement_timeout. The service now applies (idempotently, at startup, NOT a versioned migration) six indexes - three COVERING (the aggregate columns the composer SUM/AVGs are `INCLUDE`d, for an Index Only Scan) plus three lookup indexes - and a per-table `autovacuum_vacuum_insert_scale_factor = 0.02` override so the visibility map stays current on the pure-insert hypertable chunks (the default 0.2 left the day's hot chunk stale before the daily TimescaleDB rollover, degrading the Index Only Scan back to heap fetches). EXPLAIN-verified on a 137 GB field store: two panels that timed out at 15 s now run in 139 ms / 514 ms with 0 heap fetches. Results-invariant perf tuning, so it deliberately does not bump the schema version or gate the viewer.
- **Darling + Lite: the Long-Running Query alert's `sp_server_diagnostics` exclusion no longer fires a false alert when the health-check session is in a non-diagnostics wait** ([#1622]) - the exclusion keyed only off the wait type (`wait_type NOT LIKE '%SP_SERVER_DIAGNOSTICS%'`), but sp_server_diagnostics also does Extended Events work, so a field session (multi39) captured in `PREEMPTIVE_XE_GETTARGETSTATE` slipped the wait-type match and fired a false Long-Running Query alert (self-resolved 36 s later; nil real impact, but the filter missed its named target). The exclusion now also matches the query text (`query_text NOT ILIKE '%sp_server_diagnostics%'`, case-insensitive and NULL-safe), catching the session regardless of the wait it is captured in. Fixed in both apps for parity, plus a pin test.

- `install-darling.ps1`: the viewer-shortcut step no longer throws when run with no loaded user profile (a Windows service, scheduled task, CI runner, or remote session) where `GetFolderPath('Desktop'/'StartMenu')` returns empty. It creates only the shortcuts whose folder resolves and skips cleanly otherwise. ([#1613])

### Documentation

- **A second stale doc comment describing behaviour that no longer exists** ([#1744]) - `QuiesceTimescaleServerOptions` carried two stacked `<summary>` elements, the first left by an earlier edit. XML docs take the LAST summary, so tooling rendered the correct one and only a human reading the file was misled - invisible to the build, the tests and the analyzers, which is why it survived several re-reads and was found by pattern-matching the file instead. Same defect as [#1739]'s, in a second place. The surviving summary keeps both rationales that matter: the timescale/timescaledb#1593 `template0` deadlock behind quiescing the scheduler for the upgrade window, and the IPv4/IPv6 loopback trap behind restoring `listen_addresses=localhost` for it.
- Darling README: added a "verify it's actually reachable" recipe for the opt-in LAN MCP/web endpoints — confirm the listener is on the LAN address (not loopback), the scoped firewall rule covers the client, and the client connects to the box IP rather than `localhost` — plus what to re-run after a reinstall. Enabling an endpoint is not the same as reaching it. ([#1616])

## [3.2.0] - 2026-07-21

### Added

- **Darling MCP: bulk add/remove monitored servers — an MCP client can now stand up FLEET monitoring conversationally** ([#1609]) - the Darling MCP server exposed read tools plus the [#1600] Custom Views and [#1608] alert-tuning writes, but no way for an MCP client (or Claude, over MCP) to add or remove the MONITORED SERVERS themselves - onboarding was WPF-Viewer-only (the Add / Manage Servers dialogs). Two new tools close that gap, the direct sibling of the alert-tuning slice: `add_servers` (BULK) takes a JSON ARRAY of server objects and, IN ORDER (sequential, mirroring [#1549]'s bulk-probe, to avoid a probe storm), validates each, connection-tests it IN the service, and saves the new+reachable ones - so "monitor these twenty servers with this login" stands up fleet monitoring in one call; `remove_server` removes one by name. **No divergent second implementation:** the probe is `DarlingServerConnector.ProbeAsync` run IN-PROCESS (the MCP host lives inside the service, which holds the network path + credentials - unlike the Viewer's dialogs, which enqueue a `test_connect` command for the service to run), the case-folded dedupe gate is the shared `ServerIdHelper.BuildStorageName` identity in a `HashSet<string>(OrdinalIgnoreCase)` exactly as the [#1549] bulk dialog uses, the SQL password is DPAPI-encrypted through the SAME `DarlingSecrets.Protect` the service decrypts with at collection time (so it round-trips), and the INSERT mirrors `StoreConfigProvider.SeedMonitoredServersAsync`'s exact column set + `server_id` identity (so a tool-written row JOINs the collected data and the service's reconcile matches it, picking it up within one sweep - no restart). Per server, `add_servers` returns `status` `added` / `duplicate` (a case-variant or exact dup of an existing or earlier-in-batch server, skipped WITHOUT a probe) / `connection_failed` (recorded, the batch CONTINUES) / `invalid` (a bad field, or Entra/MFA/Service-Principal/Managed-Identity auth - interactive MFA is nonsensical headless, the same belt [#1549] applies), and the whole call returns `{added, skipped, failed, results:[...]}`; per Erik, the TLS options `encrypt_mode` (Optional/Mandatory/Strict) and `trust_server_certificate` are EXPOSED so a headless caller sets the connection posture explicitly. **Security (deliberate, scoped):** the tools connect as the least-privilege `mcp` role, now granted - via role PROVISIONING, not a migration - INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE on `config.config_monitored_servers` (mirroring how [#1600]/[#1608] granted their single tables), a single non-secret-KEY table: the `encrypted_password` column stays in the fail-closed secret carve, so `mcp` can WRITE a credential blob (onboarding) but can never READ one back, and it still cannot reach the `config_command` service-credential pivot or a schema-wide config write. **The #1608 beacon grant already covers this:** a `config_monitored_servers` write fires the existing `trg_bump_monitored_servers → config_bump_version` trigger (SECURITY INVOKER, UPDATEs `config_service.config_version` AS `mcp`), and [#1608] already granted `mcp` UPDATE on the two `config_service` beacon columns - verified, no new `config_service` grant is added. **Credential on the wire:** the SQL password travels to the MCP endpoint inside `add_servers`' request JSON (DPAPI-encrypted at rest, never returned by any read tool), which is one more reason a LAN deployment should front the endpoint with the documented TLS reverse proxy - the README's MCP blast-radius section now states this and recommends Windows/integrated auth for onboarded servers where possible. The MCP server instructions (now eighty-seven tools), the cross-app tool-inventory ratchet (Lite is a single-instance app with no central monitored-server store, so these are Darling-only), the `/api/read` write-exclusion set, and the Darling README are updated. Verified: the Darling service + `Darling.Tests` build clean (0 errors, no new warnings in the changed source), full `Darling.Tests` **2847 passed / 0 failed / 146 gated-live skipped** (with `DARLING_TEST_PG` cleared), and `Lite.Tests` `CrossAppMcpToolInventoryPinTests` **2 passed** - the ungated pins cover the two-tool surface, the Gemini-clean schema + required-params, validate-before-write (a malformed payload or a bad/MFA entry returns `invalid` WITHOUT probing or opening a connection), and the pure case-folded dedupe partition; one gated-live test (`DARLING_TEST_PG`, own-scoped + cleaned up, the SQL probe stubbed to success - no live SQL Server touched in CI) proves the store INSERT (the SQL secret is DPAPI-encrypted at rest and round-trips, the Windows-auth server is secret-free), the duplicate skip, the `config_version` self-bump, and `remove_server` (removed, then not_found). The REAL end-to-end probe is what the human dogfoods (remove sql2016, re-add via MCP).

- **Darling MCP: alert-tuning write tools — an MCP client can now tune thresholds and manage mute rules conversationally** ([#1608]) - the Darling MCP server exposed alert READS (`get_alert_history` / `get_alert_settings` / `get_mute_rules`) but no way for an MCP client (or Claude, over MCP) to CHANGE the alerting - thresholds and mute rules could only be edited in the WPF Viewer's Settings window. Three new tools close that gap, the direct sibling of the [#1600] Custom Views MCP write tools: `update_alert_settings` (a PARTIAL update of the single global alert-settings row - the agent reads via `get_alert_settings`, changes fields, and sends only those back in the SAME nested shape, e.g. `{"cpu":{"threshold_percent":90},"cooldown_minutes":10}`), and `create_mute_rule` / `delete_mute_rule` (add/remove the mute rules the delivery paths honor). **No divergent second implementation:** `update_alert_settings` validates EVERY provided field against the SAME ranges/enums the Viewer's Settings window enforces (`SettingsWindow.BuildAlertRowFromControls` - thresholds in range, `cpu.mode` `sql`/`total`, `delivery.mode` `Summary`/`PerEvent`, counts within bounds) BEFORE any write - an out-of-range value or an unknown field (top-level or nested) returns `{status:"invalid"}` and writes nothing - then applies ONLY the provided columns via a targeted parameterized `UPDATE ... WHERE id = 1` and re-reads the merged state; `create_mute_rule` / `delete_mute_rule` reuse the SAME `PgMuteRuleStore` `get_mute_rules` reads through, with the same GUID id-generation the Viewer's mute-create path uses. SMTP/webhook delivery credentials are out of scope (the `mcp` role cannot read or write the secret columns). A `config_alert_settings` write self-bumps `config_version` via the existing config-table trigger, so the running service HOT-RELOADS the change within one collection sweep (the tool never writes `config_version` itself). **Security (deliberate, scoped):** the tools connect as the least-privilege `mcp` role, now granted - via role PROVISIONING, not a migration - INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE on `config.config_mute_rules` and UPDATE on the singleton `config.config_alert_settings` (mirroring how [#1600] granted `config.custom_views`), so a token-holder can tune alerting but still cannot reach the `config_command` service-credential pivot or the carved secret columns. **One non-obvious grant, called out for review:** the `config_alert_settings` bump trigger (`config_bump_version`) is SECURITY INVOKER and UPDATEs `config_service` AS the writing role, so the `mcp` role ALSO needs a COLUMN-level UPDATE on just the two `config_service` beacon columns (`config_version`, `updated_at`) or every `update_alert_settings` write would fail 42501 in production - and the superuser-run gated-live tests would never catch it. The column grant lets `mcp` bump the reload beacon but NOT flip `paused` / `capture_plans` / `mcp_enabled` / `mcp_port`; the live security test now proves this end-to-end as the real `mcp` role (a `config_alert_settings` UPDATE succeeds and fires the beacon; a `paused` UPDATE still 42501s). The MCP server instructions, the cross-app tool-inventory ratchet (Lite has no central alert store, so these are Darling-only), and the Darling README's MCP blast-radius section are updated. Verified: the Darling service + `Darling.Tests` build clean (0 errors, 0 warnings in the changed source), full `Darling.Tests` **2821 passed / 0 failed / 145 gated-live skipped** (with `DARLING_TEST_PG` cleared), and `Lite.Tests` `CrossAppMcpToolInventoryPinTests` **2 passed** - the ungated pins cover the six-tool surface, the Gemini-clean schema + required-params, and validate-before-write (a bad/unknown partial update returns `invalid` WITHOUT opening a connection); one gated-live test (`DARLING_TEST_PG`, own-scoped + restored) proves `update_alert_settings` flips a threshold AND self-bumps `config_version`, and `create_mute_rule`→`get_mute_rules`→`delete_mute_rule` round-trips, and it skips in the normal unit run like the others.

- **Darling: headless `--enable-mcp` / `--disable-mcp` / `--enable-web` / `--disable-web` CLI verbs — bring an endpoint up (store + firewall) without the Viewer** ([#1601]) - a headless Darling box had no supported way to (a) turn the MCP or web-dashboard endpoint on/off or (b) open its firewall. Two structural reasons: `mcp.enabled`/`web.enabled` in `darling.json` are only a FIRST-RUN seed - after the first run the store (`config.config_service.mcp_enabled`/`web_enabled`) is authoritative and is normally toggled only by the WPF Viewer's Settings, which a headless deployment does not have; and the service runs as a virtual service account (`NT SERVICE\PerformanceMonitor Darling`) that CANNOT modify Windows Firewall, so its best-effort self-reconcile silently fails. Each verb closes both gaps in one elevated action. **(store)** a TARGETED `UPDATE config.config_service SET <flag> = ..., updated_by = 'cli' WHERE id = 1` flips ONLY that endpoint's flag; the existing BEFORE-UPDATE self-bump trigger increments `config_version`, so the worker HOT-RELOADS within one collection sweep - no restart - and the write deliberately never touches `config_version` itself, `paused`, or the other endpoint's flag (0 rows affected ⇒ the store isn't seeded yet, reported as such; the owner credential missing ⇒ the service has never initialized the store, reported as such). **(firewall)** only when the endpoint's `darling.json` network block opts into LAN exposure (a non-loopback `listen`, decided via the shared `DarlingNetwork.IsExposedListenAddress`): run ELEVATED, it opens/removes the SAME scoped, idempotent-by-DisplayName rule the host self-reconciles (the two `McpFirewallRuleName`/`WebFirewallRuleName` builders are now `internal` so the CLI and host act on the EXACT same rule, through the shared `BuildFirewallEnableCommand`/`BuildFirewallDisableCommand` builders); run NON-elevated, the store toggle still succeeds and the exact elevated command is printed as a HANDOFF (never a failure); a loopback-only endpoint gets a note pointing at `--configure-network` and takes no firewall action. A firewall failure is non-fatal. Managed-mode only (BYO governs its own `config_service` + exposure) and Windows-only (DPAPI credential decrypt + `WindowsPrincipal` + firewall), the same guard shape as `--print-viewer-connection`; wired into `IsKnownVerb`, `UsageText`, and the `Program` dispatch (allow-list and dispatch kept in sync per the existing anti-drift comments). The output states the store change, that the running service applies it live within one sweep, the firewall outcome/handoff, and a reminder that `darling.json`'s `enabled` is only the seed - the store is the live switch. No store schema change. Verified: `Darling.Tests` **2789 passed / 0 failed / 144 gated-live skipped** (with `DARLING_TEST_PG` cleared) and 0 new build warnings - the pure tests pin the four store-write SQL strings (right flag, `updated_by='cli'`, `WHERE id=1`, never `config_version`/`paused`/the other endpoint's flag), the verb recognition + classify wiring, the pure firewall-step classifier (exposed × elevated), and the shared rule names; one gated-live test (`DARLING_TEST_PG`, transaction-rolled-back) proves enable then disable flip the flag AND self-bump `config_version` against a throwaway Postgres, and it skips in the normal unit run exactly like the other `*_AgainstDevPostgres` tests (CI's `darling-pg` job runs it live).
- **Darling MCP: `describe_custom_view_catalog` — the compose vocabulary an LLM needs to author a view without guessing** ([#1602]) - [#1600] gave the Darling MCP server the seven Custom Views authoring tools, but an MCP client had no way to DISCOVER what a composed panel may contain. The tool descriptions give the panel SHAPE (a `source` + `measure`/`ratio` + `aggregate` + `unit` + `timeBucket`/`topN` + `groupBy`/`filters` + `viz`) and say "iterate until valid", but no tool enumerated the legal identifiers, and `validate_custom_view`'s errors name the first problem without listing valid values (`"unknown source 'X'"` never says what sources exist). So an assistant asked to "make me a top-waits view" had to GUESS `source`/`measure` names blind and flail on validation errors — the exact wall hit while dogfooding the [#1600] tools, cleared only by reading `MeasureCatalog.cs` source, which a customer's assistant cannot do. A human in the web composer gets dropdowns; the LLM got nothing. **Fix:** a new read-only `describe_custom_view_catalog` tool returns the SAME compose catalog the web composer's picker binds to (`DarlingWebEndpoints.BuildComposeCatalogNode`, already served at `/api/catalog`) — `{measures, dimensions, annotationSources, universalDimensions, unitFamilies, aggregates, timeBuckets, filterOps, viz}`, each measure carrying its `source`, `kind` (scalar/ratio), `validAggregates`, `allowedDimensions`, unit family + default/native unit, and per-server-type `appliesTo`. An LLM calls it ONCE and composes a valid panel on the first try instead of guessing. The MCP server instructions now direct clients to call it FIRST before authoring, and it joins the read-only `/api/read` exclusion set (like the other Custom Views tools it is served by its own richer endpoint, not the `/api/read/{tool}` 1:1 mirror). **No divergent second implementation:** the tool returns the one `BuildComposeCatalogNode` the web UI already serves — no parallel catalog. Static reference data: it reads no monitored server and no collected data, needs no store connection, and is not a write, so it does not widen the MCP token's blast radius. Verified: full `Darling.Tests` green (2768 passed / 0 failed / 143 gated-live skipped locally; the one local failure is the pre-existing timezone-sensitive viewer display test, green on UTC CI), including the updated tool-surface / Gemini-schema / required-param pins (now eight Custom Views tools), the `/api/read`-parity reflection test, and a new pin that the tool surfaces a known measure with its composable fields.

- **Darling MCP: create and manage Custom Views (CV2) over MCP** ([#1600]) - the Darling MCP server previously exposed ~72 READ tools but no way for an MCP client (or Claude, over MCP) to build a Custom View programmatically - views could only be authored in the web viewer's editor. Seven new tools close that gap and add the server's ONE write surface: `list_custom_views` / `get_custom_view` (read the saved dashboards + notebooks), `validate_custom_view` (dry-run a definition against the measure catalog + composer rules WITHOUT persisting), `create_custom_view` / `update_custom_view` (validate THEN persist; optimistic-concurrency on update), `delete_custom_view`, and `run_custom_view_panel` (compile + run a single composed panel and return `{sql, rows, annotations}` - the composer's live preview, so a generated view can be checked end-to-end). Two payoffs: ask Claude to "make me a view of X" and it builds it, and Claude authoring views exercises the CV2 catalog / spec validation / render path end-to-end - exactly where CV2 gaps hide. **No divergent second implementation:** every tool routes through the EXISTING authority - persistence is the same `CustomViewStore`, validation is the same `DarlingWebEndpoints.ValidateDefinition` (through `ComposeSpec.TryParsePanel` and the hand-authored measure catalog), and the panel run is the SAME compile-and-run extracted from the web `/api/compose/run` into a shared `RunComposedPanelAsync` that now backs both surfaces; create/update ALWAYS validate before persisting, so a stored view can always compile and render. **Security (deliberate, scoped):** the tools connect as the least-privilege `mcp` role, now granted INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE on ONLY `config.custom_views` (mirroring the `viewer` role's single-table grant for the web composer) via role PROVISIONING, not a migration - a token-holder can author custom views but still cannot reach the `config_command` service-credential pivot or the carved secret columns, and a composed query names only `collect.*` collector tables so a stored view structurally cannot read a config control-plane table. The README's MCP blast-radius section is updated to state the widened write. Verified: full `Darling.Tests` green (see the PR body for the exact pass/fail counts), including the provisioning-grant pins, the tool-validation envelopes, and a gated-live round-trip through the tools against a real PostgreSQL.


- **Darling Web: Custom Views v2 - QS execution-weighted average + per-server measure availability greying** ([#1584]) - closes the two remaining #1563 deferrals so nothing's parked. **QS execution-weighted average:** a new `Weighted` ratio mode compiles `SUM(value * weight) / NULLIF(SUM(weight), 0)` - the correct execution-weighted mean across Query Store's pre-aggregated per-interval averages, NOT an avg-of-avgs - shipping `qs_avg_duration_us` and `qs_avg_cpu_us` (the QS avg column x `execution_count`), with both operand columns pinned to the collector's `PayloadColumns`. **Per-server measure auto-greying (D4):** `/api/fleet` cards now carry the reliable per-server platform (`engine_edition` + `is_azure_sql_db` / `is_azure_mi`, from the probed servers registry), and the composer GREYS (disables + a reason) measures a SINGLE selected `$server` can't collect (Azure SQL DB / MI / on-prem, matched to the measure's `appliesTo`); an already-chosen measure stays selectable (re-scoping never silently drops a panel's metric), and All / multi-select or an unprobed server keeps the Wave-1 badges. AWS RDS + msdb deliberately stay badge-only (no reliable per-server signal without a new store column). Wired into both the dashboard and notebook composers. All additive; full `Darling.Tests` green (2677 passed / 0 failed / 141 gated-live skipped).

- **Darling Web: Custom Views v2 - notebook mode (investigation documents)** ([#1583]) - the final #1563 follow-up: an investigation / runbook document that interleaves prose with LIVE composed-panel cells, read top-to-bottom (vs the dashboard's grid for watching) - the "Datadog notebooks" half of the original ask. A notebook is a view `kind` in the SAME `config.custom_views` store: `{ kind:"notebook", cells:[ {type:"markdown",text} | {type:"panel", <the v2 composed panel spec> } ], variables, range }` - reusing the v2 panel spec, `/api/compose/run`, the render path, the store + CRUD, and the view-level `$server`/`$database`/time scope. Each panel cell is validated by the EXACT `TryParsePanel` authority (all the v2 safety); markdown cells cap at 32 KB; `ValidateDefinition` dispatches on `kind` (the dashboard `{panels}` path byte-for-byte unchanged); and the `/api/views` list summary now carries `kind` (a `definition->>'kind'` scalar, never the body) so the sidebar badges + routes notebooks with no extra fetch. **The one genuinely-new component is a minimal, air-gapped, XSS-safe Markdown -> DOM renderer** (`markdown.js`): ATX headings, paragraphs, bold/italic, inline + fenced code, ordered/unordered lists, and links - EVERY node built via `textContent` (NO innerHTML anywhere, no library), so raw markup in the source is inert BY CONSTRUCTION (a `<script>`, `onerror=`, or stray tag can only become text), and ONLY an `http(s)` href becomes an `<a>` (rel="noopener noreferrer"; javascript:/data:/vbscript:/protocol-relative/relative all fall back to literal text) - pinned by a 25-assertion payload-inert test. Ships with **4 investigation templates** (blocking-chain RCA, deadlock post-mortem, "what changed at 2am", memory/OOM), each seeded with prose + panels + event annotations referencing real catalog measures (validated against the live catalog). Notebook export/import has parity with dashboard views. The notebook composer reuses the exact dashboard panel composer (extracted, dashboard path unchanged). All additive - dashboards + v1 read behavior unchanged; air-gap + textContent discipline intact. Full `Darling.Tests` green (2665 passed / 0 failed / 141 gated-live skipped).

- **Darling Web: Custom Views v2 follow-ups (wave 2) - event annotations + per-panel drill-down** ([#1582]) - second follow-up wave on the v2 composer (#1579). **Event annotations:** a time-series composed panel can overlay event markers from up to 4 sources (deadlocks, blocked-process reports, long-query completions, default-trace events, system_health) - vertical markers at each event's time in a per-source palette (disjoint from series + severity colors), a source/label/local-time tooltip, and a small count-bearing key. Backend: a catalog of annotation sources (each pinned by test to a real collector event table's time + label columns), a `CompileAnnotations` path running one bounded parameterized query per source (identifiers from the annotation catalog ONLY, schema-qualified `collect.<table>`, the panel's window + server scope bound as `$1/$2` + `= ANY($n)`, `ORDER BY ts LIMIT 200`, on the viewer pool + statement_timeout), an additive `annotations` field on `/api/compose/run`, and `annotationSources` in `/api/catalog`; the panel spec gains an `annotations` array (0-4 known keys, deduped, time-series panels only - rejected on ranked/scalar so a stored def stays renderable). **Per-panel drill-down:** clicking a ranked bar / pie slice / legend entry pins that mark's group value as a transient `eq` filter and re-runs the panel - a "Filtered: dim = value" chip with a clear (x) pops back, and a `server` dimension also offers "Open server page". Frontend-only, through the existing validated filter path (`TryParsePanel`), nothing persisted server-side. All additive - v1 read + v2 compose behavior unchanged; XSS discipline (textContent only) + air-gap intact. Full `Darling.Tests` green (2641 passed / 0 failed / 141 gated-live skipped).

- **Darling Web: Custom Views v2 follow-ups (wave 1) - gauge ratios, stacked-bar, threshold lines, measure availability** ([#1580]) - the first wave of #1563 follow-ups on the v2 composer (#1579), rounding out its deferred list. **Gauge ratios:** a new AVG-mode ratio (`AVG(a)/NULLIF(AVG(b),0)`, for gauge operands whose SUM is meaningless) adds the derived metrics the sum-ratio couldn't express - **query-memory grant used %** (used/granted) and **single-use plans %** - both shown as a percent. (The Query Store execution-weighted average stays deferred: it needs a weighting/product-sum primitive, and a wrong avg-of-avgs is worse than a missing one.) **Stacked-bar** joins the chart set (line/area/stacked/bar/pie/stat/table) - a time-series stacked bar (one segmented bar per bucket, reusing the stacked-area stack math), offered only for a time-series-with-group spec (same coherence as stacked-area). **Threshold reference lines:** a composed panel can carry up to 4 render-only reference values (in its unit), drawn as dashed guides - horizontal on the value-axis time charts, vertical on the ranked bar - skipped (never clamped) when out of the chart's domain; validated at write time but NEVER compiled into SQL. **Measure availability:** the `/api/catalog` compose node now carries each measure's per-server-type availability (`appliesTo` {onPrem, azureSqlDb, azureMi, awsRds, needsMsdb}), derived from the owning collector's real `AppliesTo` gate, and the composer badges a constrained measure ("Not on Azure SQL DB", "Needs SQL Agent (msdb)") so a user knows where it applies. (Auto-greying by the view's actual servers is a further follow - `/api/fleet` exposes no per-server edition/type yet.) All additive - v1 read + v2 compose behavior unchanged; backend + frontend, air-gap intact. Full `Darling.Tests` green (2618 passed / 0 failed / 141 gated-live skipped).

- **Darling Web: Custom Views v2 - compose your own metrics, filters, grouping, units, and chart types** ([#1579]) - #1576 shipped custom views as a picker over ~69 fixed reads with four viz types; this rebuilds the panel model into a real compose-your-own metric/dashboard model (the Datadog-notebook / Grafana-dashboard bar) while reusing the whole #1574 web host + security stack and the #1576 stored-view CRUD. Both panel kinds coexist per-panel: a panel is COMPOSED when it names a `source`, a v1 read when it names a `read`. **Curated measure catalog** (`Compose/MeasureCatalog.cs`): the collector schema carries only {name,type,precision,scale}, so unit/archetype/dimension are a hand-authored, TEST-PINNED layer over `CollectorCatalog.PayloadColumns` (every catalog (table,column) is pinned to a real collector column, each measure's time column to the collector's `PrefixTimeColumnName`) - ~40 measures across the queryable collector tables, each tagged one of four ARCHETYPES (cumulative w/ delta, delta, gauge, per-event) that decide which aggregations are legal and which column is aggregated (SUM sums the DELTA of a cumulative counter, never the raw counter; a gauge is never summed over time; `percentile_cont` only on per-event), plus native unit + unit family + allowed dimensions, plus same-source RATIOS (the correct execution-weighted average-per-execution = SUM(delta)/NULLIF(SUM(delta),0), signal-wait %, latch avg wait, file avg read latency). **Safe query compiler** (`Compose/ComposeCompiler.cs`) turns a validated spec into PARAMETERIZED Postgres: identifiers come ONLY from the catalog and are emitted schema-qualified `collect.<table>` (a composed query can never name a config table or an off-catalog column, and never rides search_path), and every value is a bound parameter (the fleet/multi-server scope as `server_name = ANY($n)`, `= ANY` / `<> ALL` filters, bound `LIKE`, the bound naive-UTC window + `LIMIT`); `date_trunc` bucketing; the #1568 object_name module join is window-bounded and server-scoped. `Compose/ComposeSpec.TryParsePanel` is the SINGLE authority the write path and the compile-run path both route through - it cross-checks every identifier-bearing field (source, measure|ratio, aggregate, unit, filters, groupBy, timeBucket, and each `$variable` reference) against the catalog and rejects anything off-catalog, so a stored definition always compiles and vice-versa. **Fleet / multi-server:** `server` is a universal dimension (server_name on every source) - filterable and group-by-able on every measure; a view carries `$server` / `$database` variables + a global time range that drive every panel at once (flip `$server` and all panels re-scope), and `$server` = All / omitted = the whole fleet (no predicate). **Chart types:** line / area / stacked-area / bar / pie / stat / table, with viz-to-shape coherence enforced server-side (a stored def can't be un-renderable) and the composer greying out viz types incoherent with the current shape (with a reason). **DoS controls** (this is a network-reachable surface): `statement_timeout` is set on the viewer + mcp roles via role PROVISIONING (not a migration - a role GUC has no probeable schema footprint, so bumping the schema version would break the connect-time version gate), plus a 90-day window ceiling, a window-times-bucket-count ceiling, forced time-bucketing, and per-panel filter/group caps. **API:** `GET /api/catalog` gains a `compose` node (measures + dimensions + unit families + viz vocab, so the composer is data-driven, never a hardcoded list); `POST /api/compose/run` compiles and runs one panel on the least-privilege viewer pool and returns `{sql, rows}` (the live preview + the read-only "view compiled SQL"). **Frontend** (vanilla ES modules, air-gapped): a per-panel composer (`editor.js`) with a category-grouped measure/ratio picker, aggregate from the measure's valid set, a Shape control (over-time / ranked / single value), group-by chips, filter rows (dimension + op + literal/multi/`$var`; LIKE only on likeable dims), a chart picker that greys incoherent viz, grain-trap guidance (a server-grain measure points to its per-database sibling), and a live WYSIWYG preview; a view-scope bar (server single/multi/All + time + variables) that re-runs every panel; and a new render path (`compose.js`) that pivots the compiler's three row shapes (time-series / ranked / scalar) into charts, formats every value in the panel's unit, and shows honest no-data / partial-fleet / error states. All untrusted text (server / object / wait names, the compiled SQL) reaches the DOM through `textContent`. **Deferred as documented follow-ups (honest limits, not silent holes):** gauge-based ratios (grant used/ideal, single-use-plan %) and a Query Store execution-weighted AVG need an avg/avg per-row compiler primitive Compose does not have yet (QS ships executions + peak duration/CPU, which ARE correct); per-server measure-availability greying needs an `appliesTo` catalog field that isn't exposed yet (the no-data + partial-fleet states cover honest degradation); stacked-bar (only stacked-area today), thresholds/annotations, per-panel drill-down, and notebook mode are named phase-2. Pinned by catalog (table,column)/time-column/gauge-non-summable/ratio-scalar tests, spec validation (off-catalog source/measure/dimension/aggregate/unit rejected, percentile-on-non-per-event rejected, LIKE-on-non-likeable rejected, undeclared-variable rejected, caps enforced), compiler safety (schema-qualified `collect.*`, all values bound, config schema excluded, #1568 window-bounded), viz-coherence accept/reject, and explicit Flow A (procedure-name LIKE on `$server` -> line) + Flow B (top-10 databases by CPU across the fleet -> bar) compile tests; the full `Darling.Tests` suite is green (2601 passed / 0 failed / 141 gated-live skipped).

- **Darling Web: custom views / notebooks — compose your own dashboards from the read catalog** ([#1576]) - the browser dashboard (#1562) gains user-authored **Custom Views**: pick any of the ~69 read-only reads, choose a visualization (table / line / stat / bandlist), set its parameters, and save a named, shareable, versioned dashboard that every seat can open — no query engine, no new SQL, just stored JSON (`{panels:[<renderPanel descriptor>]}`) driven through the SAME `renderPanel` seam the built-in pages use. **Store (migration V31):** `config.custom_views` (name-unique, `definition` jsonb, an in-place `version` column for optimistic concurrency, UTC timestamps, `updated_by`) with a NARROW `GRANT INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE ON config.custom_views` to the least-privilege VIEWER role (mirroring the mcp role's exactly-two-table precedent — NOT an admin pool on the network-reachable web host, and no secrets in the table so no config pivot); `tools/provision-roles.sql` carries the grant for BYO stores (re-run after V31). **API:** `GET /api/views` (bare-array summaries), `GET /api/views/{id}` (full, definition embedded), `POST`/`PUT`/`DELETE`, plus `GET /api/catalog` (the read names taken from the endpoint dispatch table so it can't drift, each enriched with hand-authored param metadata naming the ACTUAL wire query keys + type/required/default) and `GET /api/session` ({can_edit}). A pure server-side `ValidateDefinition` is the authority on every write: each panel's read must be on the allowlist, its viz in the vocabulary, span 1 or 2, raw `path`-mode rejected, size/panel-count capped — a malformed dashboard can never be stored. **Editing is open to any authenticated seat** — the same reach as viewing, because networked operation is the normal way this is used: a network caller has already passed the token→cookie + CIDR gate and a loopback caller is on the host, and the NARROW single-table grant keeps every write bounded to `config.custom_views` (cross-site forgery is blocked by the always-on Host-header allowlist, the SameSite=Strict cookie, and the `application/json` requirement below). Update carries the edited version and `409`s on a stale write (or a duplicate name) rather than silently clobbering. **Frontend** (vanilla ES modules, no framework, no build step, air-gapped — the CI self-containment scan covers the new files): a **Views** list + sidebar section; a **renderer** that tiles a view's panels into a responsive `.panel-grid` through the unmodified `renderPanel` (per-panel isolation is free — each panel error-strips its own fetch/throw), pre-checking every panel's read against the catalog and viz against the registry so a stale read shows a clean "unknown read" strip instead of an opaque 404; and a **composer** — a read picker grouped by catalog category, typed parameter inputs from the catalog (int/double → number, bool → checkbox, text → input, `server` → a fleet dropdown; a required parameter blocks preview + save until filled), a viz picker, width (span 1|2), title, and a per-viz field sub-editor **seeded by client-side derivation** from a live sample fetch (infers the row set, columns/series/stats, and a format per field from the key name + sample value, reusing the util.js formatter registry) then hand-tunable, all behind a LIVE debounced preview through the real `renderPanel`; panels reorder with up/down buttons (a11y, not drag). **Export** is a runtime Blob download of the view JSON and **Import** pastes it back (client-validated against the catalog, then re-validated server-side); both gate on `can_edit`, which is true for any authenticated seat and false only if the session probe itself fails (hiding the affordances until a reload). All user text (view names, descriptions, parameter values, titles) reaches the DOM through `textContent` (never `innerHTML`), and a series color is constrained to an `<input type=color>` (#rrggbb) or the chart palette — never free text — so it can't reach the chart's style-attribute sink. The 60s background poll deliberately skips re-rendering the composer route so an in-progress edit is never clobbered. Pinned by a pure client-side derivation/validation contract plus the backend's migration-shape, catalog-parity, validation, CRUD-contract, loopback-decision, and gated live round-trip (create → get → list → update → stale-409 → dup-409 → delete-404) + grant-proof tests. **Security-review hardening (same PR):** the DNS-rebinding Host-header allowlist now runs on EVERY request in BOTH modes (it previously guarded network mode only, leaving the write path reachable cross-origin via a rebind); the mutation routes (POST/PUT/DELETE) now require `Content-Type: application/json` and 415 otherwise, forcing a CORS preflight the dashboard never answers so a simple-request CSRF can never reach a write (the frontend already sends it, and now declares it on the bodyless DELETE too); and a stored series `color` is validated server-side to a `#rrggbb` hex literal (with a shared renderer-side `normalizeColor` fallback so a bad color can never reach the chart's `style="background:..."` sink) so an imported or crafted definition can't smuggle a `url(...)` value that would beacon off-origin and defeat the air-gap. Pinned by extended `ValidateDefinition` color cases, `IsJsonContentType`/`IsHexColor` unit theories, and loopback-mode Host-allowlist cases; the #1562 network-mode auth path is unchanged. **Composer UX pass (same PR):** the shared renderer now guards a field-less table/line/stat panel (missing `columns`/`stats`/`series`) with a "No fields configured" strip instead of throwing a raw `.map`-on-undefined at every seat, and the composer auto-derives a field-config on save (with a backstop) so an imported/older-JSON panel can't be stored field-less; the panel editor gains a two-column config-plus-sticky-preview layout with progressive disclosure (identity row first, then Parameters -> Fields -> Preview), a clearer section hierarchy, a re-detect-vs-auto-detect field button with a one-line helper, a scannable panel-header echo, and a wider Title field; and the Custom Views list replaces its empty strip with a first-run hero (pitch + New view + Import + starting-point ideas) and enriches each card with a panel count + viz-type chips.

- **Darling Web: a read-only browser dashboard served by the service** ([#1574]) - Darling gains an optional web UI so an operator can watch the fleet from any browser (a remote Mac included) with no WPF viewer install. A SECOND, independent Kestrel host (`DarlingWebHostService`) serves it on its own port (default **5153**), with its own kill switch, LAN-exposure block, firewall rule, and least-privilege VIEWER-role store pool - deliberately separate from the MCP host (which owns root on 5152 and gates a different blast radius, so the two get independent enable switches and tokens). It is **off by default** and toggled LIVE from the viewer's Settings via a `web_enabled`/`web_port` control-plane pair (the twin of MCP's, added by store migration **V30**); the supervisor starts/stops/rebinds the host within seconds with no service restart. Loopback is tokenless; managed-mode LAN exposure rides a `?token=` -> HMAC-signed HttpOnly SameSite=Strict session-cookie handshake behind an in-app CIDR check, fail-closed. The HTTP surface is `GET /api/read/{tool}` 1:1 with the read-only MCP tool catalog (**69 endpoints**), calling the SAME public static tool methods the MCP server exposes so there is zero SQL/projection drift, plus a pre-banded `GET /api/fleet` NOC roll-up from a new service-side `DarlingFleetReader` that runs bounded cross-server aggregates (a fixed ~9 round-trips regardless of fleet size, not an N-servers x per-metric fan-out) and a matching `get_fleet_overview` MCP tool. The per-server health banding is unified ONCE in `PerformanceMonitor.Common` (`ServerHealthClassifier` + `ServerHealthThresholds` - collection freshness, the six per-metric bands, and the fleet band/worst-first score); the WPF viewer's Overview cards now delegate to it, so the thresholds that used to live twice can no longer drift. The browser frontend is vanilla ES modules with NO framework, NO build step, and ZERO off-origin references (air-gapped - a CI scan fails the build on any CDN / font / remote include): a **Fleet Overview** (pre-banded server cards with a status dot, six metric bands, and the amber "Awaiting first collection" state rendered exactly as the API reports it; a worst-first Needs-Attention list; an all-healthy line), a per-server **drill-down** (Overview stats, Wait Stats + a trend for the heaviest wait, Active Queries, a 4-series CPU chart, Memory and File-I/O trends, and Collection Health - all charted with dependency-free inline SVG), and a fleet-wide filterable **Alert History**. All untrusted text (query text, object / wait / server names) renders through `textContent` (never `innerHTML`), and every naive-UTC timestamp is localized in the browser. Everything renders through a `renderPanel({read, params, viz, span})` seam (viz registry: table / line / stat / bandlist; pages are hardcoded descriptor arrays) so #1563 can later drive the pages from stored JSON. A follow-up design-review pass then hardened the frontend: chart series use a neutral, severity-safe categorical palette (never the ok/warn/err alert colors) on nice-rounded, percentage-clamped axes with vertical gridlines, and the CPU chart drops Idle% (now three series) so the real series are not crushed; Alert History collapses channel/sent/muted/delivery-error into one status column and truncates each detail to a one-line click-to-expand disclosure (structured findings render as labeled Story/Severity/Confidence fields); the fleet cards gain a secondary detail line per metric (SQL-vs-total CPU, threads free/max, memory + buffer-pool GB, blocking max wait, deadlock last-seen, collectors healthy/failing), a keyboard path with focus rings, and non-color severity badges; and a fleet status-bar footer, a card sort control, tabular numerals, and assorted density/typography fixes round it out (the pre-banded card DTO gains a `deadlock_last_seen` field).

- **Long-running query completion trace (opt-in, default OFF) in Lite and the Darling viewer** ([#1572]) - a new `long_query_completions` collector captures the Extended Events equivalent of the classic long-query trace (SSMS's QuickSessionStandard completion trio): `rpc_completed` + `sql_batch_completed` filtered by `duration >= threshold` (default 2,000,000 µs = 2 s, matching the Full Dashboard's SQL Trace default in `install/30_collect_trace_management.sql`), plus `attention` captured UNFILTERED so cancelled/timed-out long queries are still evidenced (the attention event's own duration is the cancellation-handling time, not the query runtime, so a duration predicate would discard exactly those rows - MS Learn-verified). Each completion carries the 9 QuickSessionStandard actions plus duration/CPU/reads/writes/row_count/`result` (Abort = the long query was killed) and the statement text. It reads its own app-managed ring-buffer XE session exactly like the blocked-process/deadlock collectors - server-scoped on on-prem/MI/RDS, database-scoped per monitored database on Azure SQL DB (#1535 pattern; `nt_username` omitted there since Azure uses Entra/SQL auth). **Erik's dedicated switch:** the collector is seeded DISABLED and opted into per fleet (a completion trace is not free on a busy server); enabling it CREATES the session on the monitored servers and disabling it DROPS the session there (the session itself is the cost - the lifecycle reconciles to the flag both ways, mirrored across both SKUs from shared session-DDL builders so the two can't drift). Both apps get a **Long Queries** tab (a grid of completed long queries + attentions, default-sorted DESCENDING by duration, aborted/attention rows tinted) with an explicit empty-state banner naming the exact switch to turn it on when the collector is disabled, plus a `get_long_query_completions` MCP read tool. Retention rides the shared 30-day collector horizon; the Darling store adds it via migration V29.

- **Darling viewer: ships as a Velopack `Setup.exe` for remote seats, like Lite and Dashboard** ([#1571]) - the standalone viewer that connects to a central Darling store from an operator's own machine now has a real install affordance: a per-user `Setup.exe` with Start Menu / desktop shortcuts, Apps & Features registration, and delta auto-updates from the releases feed, on its own `darlingviewer` Velopack channel. This required making the viewer a first-class Velopack app (it never was - it only shipped co-located inside the service zip): a `Program.Main` entry point that calls `VelopackApp.Build().Run()` before any window (wired via `<StartupObject>`, exactly as Lite/Dashboard), the `Velopack` package pinned to the suite's `1.2.0`, and Lite's "Check for Updates" affordance ported into the About dialog (Velopack self-update, falling back to opening the releases page). Nightly builds publish the `Setup.exe` UNSIGNED as a plain release asset alongside the existing zips (which are unchanged); the release pipeline is wired to publish self-contained, sign, `vpk pack`, and upload to the channel - but the signed release path is inert until the `DarlingViewer` SignPath artifact-configuration slug is created (the standing #1340 signing prerequisite, Erik's side). The co-located viewer inside `PerformanceMonitorDarling-*.zip` is untouched (zero-config parent-directory `darling.json` discovery), and the Velopack install id (`%LocalAppData%\PerformanceMonitorDarlingViewer`) does not collide with the viewer's per-user state (`%APPDATA%\PerformanceMonitorDarling`), so both can coexist for one user.

- **Queries: Top Queries by Duration now attributes each statement to its named module â€” or "ad hoc" â€” in Lite and the Darling viewer** ([#1569]) â€” the Full Dashboard has long tied ad-hoc-looking `dm_exec_query_stats` rows back to the procedure/function/trigger they belong to (its `install/10` collector stamps `object_name`/`schema_name` onto `collect.query_stats` by joining `collect.procedure_stats` on `sql_handle`), but Lite and the Darling viewer never surfaced it â€” every Top Queries row read as an anonymous statement. Both apps now do the stitch at READ time (Option B â€” zero collector/schema changes, and it retroactively attributes already-collected data): the Top Queries by Duration reader LEFT JOINs `query_stats.sql_handle` to `procedure_stats.sql_handle` â€” the SAME normalized `CONVERT(varchar(130), ..., 1)` handle text both collectors already persist â€” and surfaces the matched module's `database.schema.object` in a new **Module** column, or the literal `ad hoc` when the handle matches no cached module. The join is deduped to ONE module identity per `sql_handle` (latest `collection_time` wins, via `ROW_NUMBER() ... WHERE rn = 1` in both the DuckDB and Postgres readers â€” Postgres has no `QUALIFY`), so a query row can never fan out across a module's many collected rows. One caveat inherent to read-time attribution: a very new module the procedure-stats collector has not captured yet will read as ad hoc until its next collection cycle. The Darling reader stays pure Postgres dialect (positional params, no T-SQL) and the additive column trips no capability-pin baseline. Same change in both apps (parity), pinned by new reader round-trip tests (Lite real-DuckDB; Darling gated live-Postgres) plus SQL-shape, PG-dialect, and row-model display pins.

- **Darling: `--configure-network` â€” a guided, fail-closed wizard for the opt-in LAN endpoints** ([#1570]) â€” opting a store or MCP endpoint onto the LAN previously meant hand-editing `darling.json`'s commented `network` templates and hoping the field shapes matched what the service enforces; a typo just silently degraded the endpoint back to loopback with a critical log line. The service exe gains `--configure-network`: an interactive wizard (Windows-only, same guard shape as `--encrypt-password` / `--print-viewer-connection`) that shows the current exposure straight from the service's OWN resolvers, walks the operator through the **store / MCP / both / disable** choices, and validates every answer **by delegation** â€” it builds the same `PostgresNetworkConfig` / `McpNetworkConfig` the service reads and runs the same `ResolveNetworkExposure` / `ResolveMcpBind` the service fail-closes on, re-prompting with the resolver's own degrade reason, so it can NEVER write a config the service would refuse. The edit is **comment-preserving text surgery** â€” a new pure `DarlingNetworkConfigEditor` classifies every character as code / string / line-comment / block-comment, so it splices the live block in without touching the sample's heavily-commented documentation (verified against the real `darling.sample.json` as a fixture, including the load-bearing "the scanner ignores the commented-out template" proof). It is gated behind `DarlingConfig.Parse` **and** a resolver re-check on the reparsed result **and** a timestamped `darling.json.bak-<timestamp>` backup, so an edit never leaves an unparseable or fail-closed file. The MCP bearer token is generated (`GeneratePassword` â†’ DPAPI `Protect` â†’ `encryptedToken`) with the plaintext printed to STDOUT exactly once and the save-this warning on STDERR; an existing token defaults to KEEP. `mcp.enabled`/`port` are control-plane after the first run, so the wizard WARNS and points at the Viewer's Settings rather than editing them (the `network` block stays file-defined + restart-only, and the Settings toggle remains the instant kill switch). It finishes with the scoped firewall command(s) and the `--print-viewer-connection` handoff, and offers an elevated restart to apply (exact `Restart-Service` / `sc.exe` commands printed when not elevated or declined). `install-darling.ps1` gains `-Network` to launch it right after the install reaches Running; the README's network section now leads with it, keeping the manual field reference beneath. Darling service only â€” no store schema change, no new NuGet packages; `RunPowerShellAsync` gains an optional `timeout` (the restart needs longer than the 30-second status budget; existing callers are byte-for-byte unaffected). Verified: the Darling service builds clean in Release and the full `Darling.Tests` suite is green (**2194 passed / 0 failed / 135 gated-live skipped**), including the pure editor surgery suite (comment survival, insert/replace/remove, comma edge cases, the mcp-section-absent create, delegated family-mismatch rejection) and the scripted-input CLI end-to-end (store write + backup + resolver-exposed, MCP token printed once + STDERR warning + DPAPI-stored, BYO refusal, disable, quit).

- **Darling: scripted install/uninstall ships in the zip â€” one elevated command instead of the manual service recipe** ([#1554]) â€” the packaged Darling zips (release and nightly) now carry `install-darling.ps1` and `uninstall-darling.ps1` at the root, riding the service's publish output like `darling.sample.json` (no packaging-workflow changes). Install: extract to the final location, run `.\install-darling.ps1` elevated â€” it verifies elevation and the exe, copies `darling.sample.json` â†’ `darling.json` and STOPS for editing on first run (never installs an unedited sample), runs the `--test-connection` pre-flight (continue-or-abort on failure; `-SkipPreflight` to skip), registers the `PerformanceMonitor Darling` Event Log source, creates the service under the `NT SERVICE` virtual account with automatic start â€” or, when the service already exists, UPGRADES it in place (stop + `sc config binPath=`, preserving `darling.json`, the store, and all credentials) â€” waits for Running, and creates Desktop + Start Menu **Darling Viewer** shortcuts (taskbar pinning is deliberately not attempted: Windows blocks programmatic pinning by design; pin from the Start Menu entry). Uninstall: stops and deletes the service and removes the shortcuts, deliberately leaving the program folder, `darling.json`, and the entire `%ProgramData%\PerformanceMonitorDarling` store/credentials/logs in place so re-running the installer fully restores the deployment; `-PurgeData` (with a typed DELETE confirmation) destroys the collected history too. The README's install section now leads with the scripted path; the manual `sc.exe` recipe remains documented beneath it.

- **Darling: a real diagnostic surface, store write-throughput headroom, and honest bootstrap status** ([#1552]) â€” tier 1 of the 24-server field-incident response (a fleet whose sequential first sweep crawled under store pressure looked exactly like servers being permanently dropped, and there was no log anywhere to say otherwise). Four changes: **(1) Rolling file log** â€” the service now writes `%ProgramData%\PerformanceMonitorDarling\logs\darling-service_yyyyMMdd.log` through a buffered, never-throwing `ILoggerProvider` (every collector run line, connect edge, reload notice, warning, and error; one file per day, 14-day retention). This is the PRIMARY surface: the Windows Event Log provider was always wired, but registering its event source requires elevation the recommended `NT SERVICE` virtual account doesn't have, so on a by-the-book install the Event Log has silently received nothing since day one. The source name is now pinned to `PerformanceMonitor Darling`, the service best-effort registers it at startup (works when anything elevated runs the exe), and the README's install steps gained the one elevated `New-EventLog` line that makes it stick. **(2) Managed store v4 conf block** â€” `max_connections = 200` and `max_wal_size = 4GB` join the append-and-heal conf discipline (PG defaults of 100/1GB are toy-sized: a fleet bootstrap's write burst forced back-to-back spread checkpoints while Windows backend-spawn churn surfaced as transient store write failures); existing clusters gain the block on their next service-owned start. **(3) "Awaiting first collection"** â€” a server with no collection EVER is now a distinct freshness band rendered amber with that exact text (card, fleet Needs-Attention, sidebar grey dot) instead of the red **Offline** overlay, because during bootstrap "queued, not reached yet" is the truth and "Offline" sent the field report chasing a phantom scheduler bug; the MCP `get_server_status` surface gains the additive `AwaitingFirstCollection` status value (existing values unchanged). **(4) PostgreSQL 18 doc truth** â€” the bundled runtime has been PostgreSQL 18.4 + TimescaleDB 2.28.1 since the last runtime bump; every doc and comment that still said "PostgreSQL 17" (README, `darling.sample.json`, workflow comments) now says 18, and the BYO recommendation reads "16 or newer, validated against 18". The scheduler-concurrency fix itself (bounded-parallel sweep with cadence jitter) is tier 2, tracked separately.

- **Nightly builds now include Darling** ([#1550]) â€” the nightly release previously carried only Dashboard, Lite, and the CLI Installer, so trying Darling on another machine meant cloning and building `dev` yourself. The nightly workflow now publishes the Darling service and viewer and packages them as `PerformanceMonitorDarling-<version>.zip` in the exact layout the stable release uses (service at the archive root with `darling.sample.json` alongside, viewer under `viewer\`, and the PostgreSQL/TimescaleDB `pg-runtime.zip` beside the service exe where first-run extraction expects it), with its SHA-256 in the existing `SHA256SUMS.txt`. The ~340MB pg-runtime fetch reuses the same content-hash cache key as the release workflow, so it downloads at most once per pin-set. Like every nightly artifact, the zip is unsigned â€” for signed builds use a stable release.

- **Add Multiple Servers: onboard a whole fleet from one pasted list, in both apps** ([#1549]) â€” adding a monitoring estate one server at a time is the onboarding wall: forty boxes meant opening the single Add-Server dialog forty times and re-typing the same SQL login into every one. Both Lite and the Darling viewer now have an **"Add Multiple Servers"** button beside Add Server that opens a paste-a-list dialog. **Grammar** (a new shared `PerformanceMonitor.Common.BulkServerListParser`, pinned by a mirrored test file in both suites so the two apps can never drift): one server per line as `server[, display name][, database]`; fields separate on a comma, or on a **tab** when the line contains one (so a spreadsheet paste keeps commas inside a field, and a display name with a comma just needs the tab form); every field is trimmed and trailing empty fields are dropped (tolerating trailing separators and Excel's trailing empty cells); blank lines and `#` comments are skipped with line numbers still reported correctly; an empty server name or a fourth field is a per-line error shown in the preview, not a silent drop. **One secret, never N** â€” the shared authentication block is entered ONCE and applied to every row: in Lite a SQL or service-principal credential mints a *single* auto-named `CredentialProfile` (unique-suffixed on a name clash) that every added server references, created only when at least one new row survives dedupe so a fully-duplicate paste leaves no orphan profile; in the Darling viewer (which has no store-side profile table) the shared credential is resolved ONCE to a single DPAPI-LocalMachine blob â€” `ViewerServerSecret.Protect` called exactly once â€” and that same blob is stamped onto every server row and every `test_connect` probe. No plaintext credential is ever written, and there is never a per-server secret to rotate. **Duplicates are skipped, not silently merged** â€” both apps gate candidates through the ratified `HashSet<string>(OrdinalIgnoreCase)` over the shared `ServerIdHelper.BuildStorageName(name, db, readOnlyIntent)` identity, seeded from the servers you already monitor and derived from the BUILT row so the dedupe key and the stored `server_id` are one composition. Case-folding is the point: an existing `SQL01` skips a pasted `sql01` even though their raw hashes differ (hostnames are case-insensitive in reality), while a per-database Azure entry (`host` vs `host, , AdventureWorks`) and a read-only-intent connection stay correctly distinct â€” this prevents both the exact-case silent-UPDATE (which would clobber an existing server's excluded-databases / capture settings through the upsert) and the case-variant double-INSERT (a second row collecting the same host). A **blank database maps to NULL, never `master`** â€” coercing it would mint a mismatched identity and split a server's collected data from its single-added twin. **Test All** is informational only (no server is added) and its shape is honest per app: Lite probes directly, so it runs up to 8 in parallel, cancellable, with each probe catching every error internally (so cancelling never crashes the app) and honoring your configured connection timeout; the Darling viewer routes probes through the service's `test_connect` command, and because the service drains its command queue serially, Test All is a **sequential** sweep with live per-row status and a Cancel that stops between rows (the in-flight probe finishes and cleans up its own credential-bearing command row â€” no orphaned rows, no parallelism theater). **Entra MFA is deliberately excluded from bulk** in both apps â€” an interactive auth popup per server is a popup storm at first collection â€” and a defensive belt in each app's row-mapping helper rejects any resolved un-honorable auth (Lite: a hand-edited MFA profile; Darling: an Azure profile the service can't connect with) at the single choke point that feeds both Test All and Add, so it can never leak in through a profile. Add writes every valid non-duplicate row (per-row failures are reported and the batch continues), reports an added / skipped / failed summary, and does not auto-connect â€” the background collector picks the new rows up on its next cycle. The single Add-Server dialog is unchanged (it remains the path for MFA and the per-server niceties bulk omits), and the deprecated Full Dashboard is untouched. Verified: Lite and the Darling viewer build with 0 errors; the full Lite.Tests and Darling.Tests suites (including the mirrored parser grammar cases, the row-mapping + dedupe-gate pins, and both apps' ThemeCompletenessTests) pass; no capability-pin baselines changed.

- **NOC Overview: sort the server tiles by CPU% descending, with a persisted CPU%/Name selector (both apps)** ([#1547]) â€” the Overview server-tile grid now leads with the busiest server instead of reshuffling itself every cycle. Lite ordered the tiles favorite-then-last-connected, so the REPORTED problem was that each 30-second collection cycle re-sorted them and you couldn't tell where anything was unless its colour band happened to change ("can't tell where things are unless they're changing colours"); the Darling viewer ordered them alphabetically. Neither surfaced the hot server. Both apps now default to sorting on `CpuPercentForAlert` DESCENDING â€” the exact value each tile's CPU colour band reads â€” so the sort order matches the tile colours, with no-CPU-sample servers (Azure, or a server that has never reported) sinking to the bottom and a deterministic tiebreak (display name case-insensitive, then a stable server id) so identical data yields the identical sequence on every refresh and the tiles never jitter. A small **Sort** selector on each Overview ("CPU %" default / "Name") switches to alphabetical; the choice persists per app (Lite `settings.json`, the Darling viewer's `viewer-settings.json`) and survives restart. The ordering is one shared, pure, total-order comparer â€” `PerformanceMonitor.Ui.ServerOverviewSort`, used by both front ends (the `ChartStyle` shared-pure-function precedent) and unit-tested identically in `Lite.Tests` and `Darling.Tests` â€” so the two apps can never drift apart. No other surface changes order: the server lists, favourites, and the fleet roll-up are untouched.

- **Front-end shell collapse: the standalone Plan Viewer's tab management hoisted to `Ui.StandalonePlanViewerController`** ([#1536]) â€” the standalone Plan Viewer surface (the closable "Plan Viewer" aggregate tab where a user opens a `.sqlplan` / plan-XML file, pastes plan XML, or drags a file in, each rendering as a closable sub-tab hosting the shared `PlanViewerControl`) carried ~300 near-verbatim lines duplicated between Lite `MainWindow.PlanViewer.cs` (425 lines) and the Darling viewer's `MainWindow.PlanViewer.cs` (467 lines): the `"__PLAN_ADD_TAB__"` "+"-tab sentinel and its `SelectionChanged` spawn, the empty "New Plan" sub-tab construction (dashed-rect empty state, the Open/Paste buttons and their file-dialog / clipboard handlers, the per-sub-tab close button), `LoadPlanIntoSubTab` (async plan render + invalid-XML / load-error dialogs + header relabel), unique sub-tab labelling, active-sub-tab tracking, the drag-over / drop / Ctrl+V-paste logic, and `IsPlanFile` â€” differing ONLY in the sentinel const name (`LitePlanAddTabId` vs `DarlingPlanAddTabId`, same VALUE) and the outer server-tabs container the entry/close touches. It collapses into a new `PerformanceMonitor.Ui.StandalonePlanViewerController` constructed with the app's inner plan `TabControl`; the two resource lookups (`ForegroundMutedBrush` / `TabCloseButton`) resolve through the injected control's logical tree, so each app's own theme dictionaries still apply â€” no visual change. Each app KEEPS only what is app-specific: the outer-container reveal/close (Lite's `ServerTabControl` + select-first-visible vs the viewer's `MainTabs` + the no-servers empty-state restore), the Darling-only `OpenStoredPlanInPlanViewer` storeâ†’viewer entry (the FinOps "View Plan" destination, fed by `FinOpsTab.PlanRequested`), and the three XAML-wired drag/drop/key handlers as one-line forwarders to the controller. A new `PlanViewerCapabilityPinTests` ratchet (in BOTH test projects, mirroring the `ServerTab` pins â€” a fixed probe table matched against the combined {app `MainWindow.xaml` + thin `MainWindow.PlanViewer.cs` + shared controller} surface: 23 probes Lite / 24 Darling incl. the storeâ†’viewer entry) guards every plan-loading / drag-drop / paste / open-file capability and every per-app XAML shell wire against a silent drop; each capability was verified present in the pre-hoist source, so the baseline proves the hoist dropped nothing. The deprecated Full Dashboard has no standalone Plan Viewer and is untouched. Verified: all three apps build with 0 errors and the capability pins stay green in BOTH Lite (7/7) and Darling (7/7). Refactor only â€” no behavior change.

Because `ServerTab` / `ViewerServerTab` are PARTIAL classes and `using static` is per-file, every partial that CALLS a helper (including the block-chain / deadlock / plans / active-queries / live-plan / blocking partials that only call it, never defined it) got its own `using static`; the call sites were left untouched. The deprecated Full Dashboard has no copies and is untouched. Verified end-to-end: all three apps build with 0 errors and the capability pin stays green in BOTH Lite (6/6) and Darling (6/6). Refactor only â€” no behavior change.
- **Front-end shell collapse â€” Batch A: `SelectableItem` hoisted to `PerformanceMonitor.Ui`** ([#1523]) â€” the first code de-duplication under the [#1522] capability pin. The 2-property picker-checkbox POCO (`DisplayName` + `IsSelected`) that Lite (`LocalDataService.WaitStats.cs`) and the Darling viewer (`ViewerDataService.Waits.cs`) each defined verbatim now lives ONCE in the shared `Ui` project; both apps' wait-type / perfmon / memory-clerk / database pickers consume it. Hoisted as-is (no `INotifyPropertyChanged` â€” neither app had it, so adding it would change behavior). The deprecated Full Dashboard keeps its own copy (`Dashboard/Models/SelectableItem.cs`, not developed further); because its two consumer files import BOTH `PerformanceMonitor.Ui` and their own `Models` namespace and reference `SelectableItem` unqualified, a one-line `using SelectableItem = ...Models.SelectableItem;` alias was added to each to avoid a CS0104 ambiguity with the newly-shared type (a build-break the hoist would otherwise introduce). Verified end-to-end: all three apps build, the capability pin stays green in BOTH Lite and Darling (no user-facing capability changed), and the Darling picker-logic tests pass against the shared type. Refactor only â€” no behavior change.
- **Front-end shell collapse â€” PR0: the capability-inventory pin (safety net before any hoist)** ([#1522]) â€” the upcoming Lite<->Darling front-end de-duplication (hoisting the large duplicated code-behind out of `Lite/Controls/ServerTab.*` and `Darling/.../ViewerServerTab.*` into the shared `Ui`/`Common` projects, behind each app's OWN unchanged tab shell) is guarded by a new RATCHET test in BOTH CI-run test projects â€” `Lite.Tests/ServerTabCapabilityPinTests` + `Darling.Tests/ViewerServerTabCapabilityPinTests` â€” so no user-facing capability can silently vanish during the refactor. WPF's generated `Connect()` only errors on a DANGLING handler reference; it does NOT catch removing an XAML wire, a dropped `new DataGridFilterManager<T>()`, chart-menu wiring, or a grid's `RowStyle` (how its whole context menu attaches) â€” those are the "went missing" modes. Each pin text-scans the SOURCE (XAML + `.cs`, NO WPF/DuckDB/Npgsql/assembly load â€” the same `[CallerFilePath]` idiom as `CollectorViewerCoverageTests`) into SIX capability SETS: top-level + sub-tab headers; the `DataGridFilterManager` set (file-qualified, INCLUDING the history/drill windows the collapse will edit, so a per-window drop is visible); the chart context-menu + drill-down wiring set (anchored on the chart FIELD NAME, not the builder method, so a later `SetupChartContextMenu`->`BuildChartContextMenu` rename does not trip it); the per-menu context-command pairs; the grid->`RowStyle`/menu map (Section E â€” the binding Section D can't see); and the grid `MouseDoubleClick`/`SelectionChanged` wires (Section F â€” scanned from BOTH XAML attributes and code-behind `+=`, since Darling wires its history double-clicks in code). Each section asserts the committed BASELINE is a SUBSET of the current source â€” a captured capability may never disappear, but NEW ones are free (no exact-golden regen churn, which a PR that both adds and drops could regenerate wholesale and mask the drop). A per-section non-vacuous floor + a scan self-check reject a broken scan; a deliberate removal is an explicit baseline edit in the same PR. Proven to fire (a bogus baseline entry reds the pin with a "capability silently went missing" message). Also corrects the stale `/// Tab N` routing doc-comments in `ServerTab.Refresh.cs` (drifted +1..+5 as tabs were inserted over time; the switch itself was always correct) so a future rebuild isn't misled. Test-only + doc-comment â€” no app behavior changes.
- **Analysis engine: plan-cache single-use bloat + ring-buffer memory-pressure issue-classification arms in the shared FactScorer (Tier-2 parity, batch-2b remainder)** ([#1521]) â€” the LAST two Tier-2 arms: the shared `PerformanceMonitor.Analysis` scorer (ONE code path for Lite, the deprecated Dashboard, AND Darling) now classifies two memory problems the deprecated Dashboard's `report.*` SQL flagged but the ported engine did not. Unlike the earlier batches these need NEW facts emitted from data that IS collected but was not yet a fact, so each is emitted from the two ACTIVE fact collectors ONLY â€” Lite `DuckDbFactCollector` + Darling `PgFactCollector` (byte-identical SQL, registered in each collector's `CollectFactsAsync` method list) â€” **the deprecated Dashboard's `SqlServerFactCollector` is untouched** (correctly never emits them). (1) **Plan-cache single-use bloat** â€” a new `PLAN_CACHE_BLOAT` fact SUMs the LATEST `plan_cache_stats` snapshot (`single_use_plans` / `total_plans` / sizes, read point-in-time via `DENSE_RANK()` â€” no `QUALIFY`, which is banned in the shared PG dialect) into `single_use_percent`, tiered to `report.plan_cache_bloat`'s `bloat_level` CASE (`install/47_create_reporting_views.sql:1485-1487`): > 50% CRITICAL-tier, > 30% HIGH, > 20% MEDIUM, behind a `single_use_size_mb >= 100` NOISE guard (this is a SCORED recommendation, not the Dashboard's raw view â€” a tiny/idle cache's high single-use % is not real memory bloat). Added to `InferenceEngine.ConfigAdvisoryRootKeys` as a standing cache-composition state; the advice card names `optimize for ad hoc workloads` + parameterization (the Dashboard's own recommendation, install/47:1493). (2) **Ring-buffer physical-memory-pressure** â€” a new `MEMORY_PRESSURE_EVENTS` fact reads `memory_pressure_events` (RING_BUFFER_RESOURCE_MONITOR / RESOURCE_MEMPHYSICAL_LOW notifications) over the analysis window and bands the max process/system indicator to `report.memory_pressure_events`'s `severity` CASE (`install/47:229-236`): indicator >= 3 HIGH (`0.9`), >= 2 MEDIUM (`0.5`) â€” the collector gates out the steady 0-1 samples every healthy server's ring buffer carries. This is a genuinely distinct signal from the existing deviation-based `ANOMALY_MEMORY_PRESSURE` (which watches `peak_memory_pressure_pct`), and â€” being incident-ish (a real event, not a standing config) â€” roots via the `0.5` incident threshold rather than a root-key. Base severity maxes at `1.0` (WARNING) like every base fact; the CRITICAL band stays reserved for corroboration. `FactScorer` tests in BOTH `Lite.Tests` and `Dashboard.Tests` pin every tier and both guards; `FactCollectorTests` round-trips both new collectors against seeded DuckDB (the fact is emitted with the right value/metadata, and ABSENT when no snapshot exists / only steady samples are present); and the Darling `PgFactCollectorTests` collect-method-surface + `AllSql` dialect/resolution pins are extended for the two new byte-identical queries. No test-seeder fix was needed â€” neither `plan_cache_stats` nor `memory_pressure_events` is seeded by any existing fixture, so both arms are silent on every prior scenario. Lands in Darling + Lite; the deprecated Dashboard is not developed further.
- **Analysis engine: two server-config issue-classification arms (priority boost + lightweight pooling) in the shared FactScorer (Tier-2 parity, batch-2b)** ([#1520]) â€” the shared `PerformanceMonitor.Analysis` scorer (ONE code path for Lite, the deprecated Dashboard, AND Darling) now classifies two dangerous `sp_configure` settings the Dashboard's `config.critical_issues` analyzer flagged as WARNINGs but the ported engine did not. Both are near-pure additions over data ALREADY collected: `server_config` accumulates the FULL `sys.configurations` set, so the two ACTIVE fact collectors (Lite `DuckDbFactCollector` + Darling `PgFactCollector`) simply add `'priority boost'` + `'lightweight pooling'` to their curated fact-emission list, emitting `CONFIG_PRIORITY_BOOST` / `CONFIG_LIGHTWEIGHT_POOLING` â€” **the deprecated Dashboard's `SqlServerFactCollector` is untouched** (it just never emits the facts). (1) **Priority boost enabled** (`install/50_configuration_issues_analyzer.sql:368`, WARNING) â€” above-normal Windows scheduling priority that starves OS-critical threads. (2) **Lightweight pooling / fiber mode enabled** (`install/50:401`, WARNING) â€” breaks OLEDB and other in-process components. Each scores the WARNING band (`0.9` in `FactScorer`) when `value_in_use == 1` â€” surfacing prominently, since these are rare, clearly-wrong settings rather than routine tuning choices like MAXDOP/CTFP (which stay at the low `0.4` config-advisory base) â€” and roots a standalone card (added to `InferenceEngine.ConfigAdvisoryRootKeys`). Sourced advice (the Dashboard messages + the `sp_configure ..., 0; RECONFIGURE;` remediation) added to `FactAdvice`. `FactScorerTests` in BOTH `Lite.Tests` and `Dashboard.Tests` pin the WARNING band when enabled and dormancy when disabled; the arms are silent on every existing fixture (no seeder enables either setting). Lands in Darling + Lite; the deprecated Dashboard is not developed further.
