One deliberate metrics nuance: the fetch/store timing split ([#1180]) now uniformly attributes result-row reading to the SQL (fetch) phase for every migrated collector â€” cpu_utilization, memory_clerks, memory_pressure_events, session_stats, waiting_tasks, and procedure_stats previously counted row reads in the storage phase as a streaming artifact, so their reported sql_ms rises and duck_ms falls correspondingly; collected monitoring data is unaffected. Batch 4 (the three memory collectors) added the last general contract piece: definitions declare `AppliesTo(target)` so collectors that don't exist on a platform skip the whole cycle (memory_pressure_events on Azure SQL DB, where ring buffers aren't exposed), and memory_stats carries the [#857] elastic-pool variant (committed-target approximation, NULL worker count) verbatim. Batch 5 (file_io_stats) added the per-database execution mode â€” Azure SQL DB scopes `dm_io_virtual_file_stats` to the connected database, so definitions can declare `RunsPerDatabase` and the host enumerates databases, opens per-database connections, and aggregates rows (erroring databases skipped and logged, as before) â€” plus the shared `DatabaseExclusionFilter` (the parameterized `NOT IN` splice), pinned by a test asserting it produces byte-identical SQL to Lite's original builder so the temporary duplication cannot drift. Batch 6 (server_properties) added best-effort **supplemental queries** â€” a definition can declare a second query on the same connection whose failure can never fail the primary rows (the WS5 LPIM/IFI/memory-dump health probe, skipped on Azure), moved the Azure vCore parser into the shared definition, and introduced `CollectorDefinitionBase` so new definitions override only what differs from the common case. Batch 7 (server_config, database_config, trace_flags) added **version-gated column selection** â€” `CollectorTargetInfo` now carries the SQL major version and the Managed-Instance flag, and database_config reproduces the original's gates exactly (2019 columns on major â‰¥ 15 / version-unknown / Azure; the 2025 optimized-locking column on major â‰¥ 17; ungated columns write NULL) â€” while trace_flags keeps its permission-tolerant DBCC catch host-side. Batch 8 (database_scoped_config) added the **enumeration shape** â€” the proven `[db].sys.sp_executesql` per-database idiom: a definition lists items first (the on-prem list keeps its AG filter, collecting only from non-AG databases or primary replicas), then runs one query per item on the same connection with bracket-escaped database names, and a failing database is skipped with a warning exactly as before. This is the execution shape the Query Store collector uses, so its extraction is now unblocked. Batch 9 (session_stats, waiting_tasks) needed no new contract shapes â€” the library's shapes now cover the majority of Lite's collection surface. Batch 10 (procedure_stats) carried the dynamic-SQL shape over intact â€” the proc/trigger/function UNION built into an `@sql` variable (spills columns spliced per branch because `dm_exec_function_stats` has none), the exclusion filter interpolated as **double-escaped literals** via the shared `DatabaseExclusionFilter.BuildLiteralClause` (identity-pinned against Lite's original in both escape modes), the Azure single-database variant, and the plan_handle-keyed delta groups with their db.schema.object fallback. Batch 11 (running_jobs) carried the msdb p95-duration comparison over verbatim and pinned the one prefix exception (`running_jobs` has no `collection_id`; definitions now declare `IncludesCollectionId`); the failed-jobs alert query deliberately stays host-side until the Phase-5 alert-engine extraction. A mid-sweep retrospective at 16/25 (mechanical SQL-fidelity audit of every extracted query against the pre-extraction originals + a fresh-context review across runner semantics, payload order, deltas, types, and exception scoping) found **zero blockers or majors**; its three small findings are folded in here: the fetch/store timing note above widened beyond cpu, the supplemental query now skips on an empty primary result exactly like the originals, and `resource_semaphore_id`'s column metadata corrected to a new `SmallInt` type (matters only for future Darling DDL generation). Batch 12 (perfmon_stats, dmv_blocking_snapshot) moved two more pieces of monitoring brain into the shared library: the **curated 59-counter perfmon list** now lives in the definition (the `perfmon_counters.json` override still wins, flowing in via context) with its escaped-literal interpolation and `{object}|{counter}|{instance}` delta contract pinned, and the DMV blocking snapshot carries its layered minimum-wait floors and the **synthetic negative monitor_loop** (seconds since 2020-01-01, negated so DMV episodes can never collide with real BPR loops) verbatim. Batch 13 (database_size_stats) needed nothing new: the server-side cursor + `FILEPROPERTY(SpaceUsed)` staging batch, the two-site exclusion splice (cursor and outer SELECT sharing one parameter set), volume stats, and the Azure per-database variant all rode existing shapes. Batch 14 (index_object_stats â€” the heavy per-index sweep) added the last small contract knob, a **per-collector command timeout** (this collector keeps its dedicated 300-second per-database budget from the [#1135] fix while enumeration stays on the default), preserved the `#temp`-staged sp_IndexCleanup technique and dual execution (Azure per-database connections; on-prem enumeration with the quote-doubled `[db].sys.sp_executesql` body), and unified the per-database loops' error handling to be cancellation-aware: a cancel now always propagates instead of being swallowed as a skipped database, and a non-SQL per-database failure now skips that database with a warning instead of failing the whole cycle (previously only SqlExceptions were tolerated on the enumeration path). Batch 15 (query_stats) preserved the full row-identity delta key (`sql_handle:start:end:plan_handle` â€” the fix for multi-statement plans cross-contaminating each other's deltas), the interval-captured worker delta feeding `sample_interval_seconds`, and both query variants; its previously-overlapping fetch/store stopwatches join the uniform timing attribution documented above. Batch 16 (query_snapshots) moved the running-queries snapshot â€” the CDC-capture detection (Agent job-id parse with the text-match fallback when msdb's cdc_jobs is unreadable), the live-query-plan version gate (2016 SP1+/MI, never Azure SQL DB â€” [#857]), the Azure `#req`-staging variant that keeps the sql_text/plan DMFs away from master-scoped rows ([#857]), and the [#1286] memory-grant/tempdb/transaction triage columns â€” into the shared definition; its query templates now also serve Lite's **live snapshot button** through a one-line shim, so the scheduled collector and the interactive view can never build different SQL. Batch 17 (query_store) rode the batch-8 enumeration shape (the actual_state cursor probe that lists only databases with Query Store genuinely enabled â€” AG-aware on-prem, plain on Azure) plus the last small contract knob, an optional **enumeration probe**: a quick best-effort scalar the host runs between listing items and the per-item loop, because query_store decides its 2017+/2022+ version-gated columns from a **live PRODUCTVERSION check each cycle** (10-second budget, default 13 on failure) rather than trusting cached connection status, which can be version-unknown. The incremental `last_execution_time` watermark (60-minute fallback), the per-database `sp_executesql` query, and the float-to-bigint stat coercion moved verbatim; the collector is the second Nameâ‰ TargetTable case (`query_store` â†’ `query_store_stats`). One deliberate deviation: the per-database DuckDB write-spike breakdown log (reader/append/flush stopwatches behind a 2-second threshold) was diagnostic scaffolding from the Query Store performance investigations and does not carry over â€” the runner's uniform fetch/store phase timing ([#1180]) remains. Batch 18 (deadlocks) opened the XE pair: the definition owns both ring-buffer reads (server-scoped `xml_deadlock_report` on-prem/MI/RDS vs the database-scoped `database_xml_deadlock_report` on Azure SQL DB), the `deadlock_time` watermark that keeps ring-buffer lingerers from re-inserting (10-minute fallback), and the victim-inputbuf extraction from the graph XML (parsed in the read phase, where its cost belongs) â€” while the XE session **lifecycle** (create/start/ensure, the #1251 benign-already-present handling) deliberately stays host-side with the session name now sourced from the definition, so the reader and the lifecycle can never disagree on it; the missing-session tolerance (errors 297/15151 â†’ zero rows) stays in the host wrapper exactly like trace_flags' permission catch. Batch 19 (blocked_process_report) **completed the sweep**: the definition owns the ring-buffer read (server- vs database-scoped DMV pair), the full sp_HumanEventsBlockViewer-mirroring `wait_resource` â†’ contentious-object resolution batch (KEY via per-database `sys.partitions`, PAGE/RID via `sys.dm_db_page_info` on 2019+/Azure, the event object_id COALESCE fallback and the `Unresolved:` sentinel â€” the machinery behind the [#1140] fingerprint agreeing across apps), the `event_time` watermark, and the C# blocked-process-report XML parse (now directly unit-tested: both processes' attributes, the root `monitorLoop`, null on garbage or a missing blocked-process) â€” while the session lifecycle **including the blocked-process-threshold `sp_configure` bootstrap** stays host-side; its previously hand-rolled DuckDB `MAX(event_time)` watermark was byte-equivalent to the shared helper and now rides the standard runner path (third Nameâ‰ TargetTable case: `blocked_process_report` â†’ `blocked_process_reports`). With the sweep complete, every query Lite sends to a monitored server, every result-row mapping, every delta rule, and every payload order lives in the shared library â€” the parity keystone the Darling headless service builds on
- **Darling: the Postgres schema is now generated from the shared collector definitions** ([#1262]) â€” first slice of milestone M1: `PgSchemaGenerator` in `PerformanceMonitor.Darling.Storage` walks the new `CollectorCatalog` (all 26 definitions, enumerable through a new engine-neutral `ICollectorSchemaInfo` surface) and emits Darling's `CREATE TABLE` / index DDL from the same column metadata Lite's DuckDB schema was hand-written against â€” so a collector change lands once and both stores stay column-for-column identical. Column names and types **mirror Lite's DuckDB schema exactly** (analysis SQL can twin without translation): the definitions now carry the prefix column names where Lite's schema varies (`deadlock_id`, `blocked_report_id`, the config snapshots' `config_id`/`capture_time`, running_jobs' no-id prefix) and per-column decimal precision (`numeric(18,2)` memory MBs, `numeric(5,2)` percent_complete, `numeric(19,2)` size stats, `numeric(10,1)` job percent-of-average â€” 36 columns audited against Lite's declared `DECIMAL(p,s)`). Two documented physical deviations: DuckDB's PRIMARY KEY on the id column is not carried over (TimescaleDB hypertables require the partition column in any unique constraint, and COPY ingest doesn't want one), and index names are uniform where Lite's handwritten ones are irregular (index *columns* mirror exactly, including memory_pressure_events' `sample_time` and index_object_stats' composite drill index; server_config/database_config stay index-less). Hypertable conversion is deliberately deferred to the migration runner, validated against a live Postgres before it ships. Covered by `PgSchemaGeneratorTests`. The second M1 slice adds the **ingest plumbing**: `PgCollectorRowWriter` adapts the definitions' positional row writes onto Postgres **binary COPY** (Darling's counterpart of Lite's DuckDB appender adapter â€” column order stays the contract), with every `DateTime` written Kind-Unspecified because the product stores naive-UTC `timestamp` mirroring DuckDB and Npgsql 6+ strictly maps Kind-Utc to `timestamptz`; `CopyCommandFor` emits each collector's COPY column list (prefix first, running_jobs' no-id honored); and `PgMigrations` is the versioned-plain-SQL migration runner from the plan (each script once, in its own transaction, tracked in `darling_schema_version`; V1 = the generated collector tables; `StorageVersion.SchemaVersion` now pinned to the latest script). An **end-to-end migrate â†’ COPY â†’ read-back test** is wired but skips unless `DARLING_TEST_PG` points at a live Postgres, so it lights up the moment a dev instance exists (validated live 2026-07-02 against a portable PG 17.10). The first M2 slice moves the remaining **collection-behavior defaults into the shared library** so the future service collects identically to Lite out of the box: the delta-calculation core becomes the shared `CollectorDeltaCalculator` (baseline / counter-reset / gap-policy semantics live once; Lite's `DeltaCalculator` now derives from it, keeping only its DuckDB restart-seeding), the 124-entry default ignored-wait-types list becomes `IgnoredWaitDefaults` (Lite still ships and loads its user-overridable json â€” an identity-pin test asserts the two cannot drift), the per-collector cadence/retention defaults become `CollectorScheduleDefaults` (same pin against Lite's ScheduleManager table), collection-id stamping becomes the shared `CollectionIdGenerator`, and the server-identity storage-name rule (`host[:database][:RO]`, hashed to server_id) moves to the shared `ServerIdHelper.BuildStorageName` â€” so a Darling install monitoring the same server derives the same server_id Lite would. The second M2 slice is the service's **config plane**: `darling.json` (resolved explicit-path â†’ `DARLING_CONFIG` env â†’ next to the binary; comments allowed) holds the Postgres store and the monitored servers â€” deliberately minimal, with **no schedule knobs** (the shared defaults apply). SQL-auth passwords are **DPAPI-LocalMachine-protected** via a new `--encrypt-password` CLI verb (reads from stdin so plaintext never lands in shell history; machine-bound blob an administrator encrypts and the service account decrypts â€” deliberately different from Lite's user-profile-scoped Credential Manager, which is wrong for a service), with plaintext accepted as a warned dev convenience; integrated auth is the recommended default. The per-server connection string mirrors Lite's posture exactly (MARS, 15-second connect budget, `Encrypt` failing closed to Mandatory on unknown modes) â€” only the ApplicationName differs â€” and a shipped `darling.sample.json` documents the shape. Covered by `DarlingConfigTests`. The third M2 slice is the **collection engine itself**: `DarlingServerConnector` probes each configured server with the verbatim Lite detection query (engine edition, major version, RDS, msdb access â€” both SKUs classify a server identically), and `DarlingCollectorRunner` is Lite's definition runner ported semantics-for-semantics onto Postgres â€” AppliesTo skip, watermarks read from Postgres, the three execution paths (per-database Azure connections with the [#857] master-inaccessible fallback and per-server caching; enumeration with the optional scalar probe; plain single query with best-effort supplemental), cancellation-aware per-item catches, separated fetch/store timing, and binary-COPY storage with the standard prefix. **Proven live end-to-end**: the gated E2E collected real wait_stats from a SQL Server 2022 through the shared `WaitStatsCollector` definition twice (baselines, then real deltas through the shared calculator with the shared ignored-wait filtering) into a live Postgres, verified row counts and the watermark â€” the same monitoring brain, two storage engines. The final M2 slice is the **collection loop itself**: `DarlingWorker` loads and validates the config, migrates the Postgres store, connects and probes each server (failed servers retried every sweep; a connection-level failure mid-cycle forces a reconnect + reprobe), ensures the XE ring-buffer sessions (the Lite lifecycle ported, including the #1251 benign-already-present handling and the RDS-tolerant blocked-process-threshold bootstrap), runs the on-load config snapshots once per connect, then runs every collector on the shared cadence via a 26-entry typed dispatch (coverage-pinned against the catalog so an unwired collector fails a test, not production) with Lite's tolerances mirrored â€” the XE readers treat a missing session as zero rows, trace_flags treats denied DBCC as zero rows. **The service ran live unattended** against a SQL Server 2022 into Postgres: all 26 collectors executed with zero errors, data landed in every expected table, second-cycle wait deltas were non-zero (the shared delta calculator working through the service), and a follow-up run picked up fresh StackOverflow2010 query activity in query_stats â€” the earlier zero was the definition's own system-database/self-marker filtering working as designed. The first M3 slice is the **V2 observability store**: a `servers` registry the service upserts on every successful connect and a `collection_log` the service writes after every collector run (SUCCESS/PERMISSIONS/ERROR, Lite's status vocabulary) â€” column names mirroring Lite's DuckDB pair so viewer/analysis SQL can twin, with `duckdb_duration_ms` recording the Postgres storage phase, and failure-isolated so an observability write can never break collection. The service now also enforces **data retention**: a daily DELETE-based purge driven by the shared per-collector `RetentionDays` (identity-pinned to Lite's horizons, plus a 30-day collection_log horizon), failure-isolated per table so one stuck DELETE can never stop the sweep, with TimescaleDB `drop_chunks` as the extension-present upgrade path (since landed â€” see the optional-TimescaleDB storage-evolution bullet below)Restart continuity: the service now re-seeds delta baselines from Postgres at startup â€” `DarlingDeltaCalculator` ports Lite's DuckDB seeding verbatim (the same four tables: wait_stats, file_io_stats, perfmon_stats, memory_grant_stats, same delta groups and key formats), so a service restart doesn't zero the first cycle's deltas â€” and the servers registry now records the real probed engine edition instead of 0 for box servers
- **Darling captures execution plans; Lite still does not** ([#1262]) â€” the shared `query_stats` and `query_store` collectors gained an opt-in `CapturePlanXml` context flag (default off) that splices execution-plan capture into the collection SQL, mirroring the full Dashboard's `@collect_plan` path verbatim: for query_stats the statement-level plan from `sys.dm_exec_text_query_plan(plan_handle, statement_start_offset, statement_end_offset)` â€” the text DMV, not `dm_exec_query_plan`, so large/deep plans that overflow the xml type still return â€” and for query_store `CONVERT(nvarchar(max), sys.query_store_plan.query_plan)`, with no size guard either place (matching install/08_collect_query_stats.sql and install/09_collect_query_store.sql). `DarlingCollectorRunner` sets the flag from a new `capturePlans` darling.json key (default true) and the captured plans land in the already-present `query_stats.query_plan_xml` / `query_store_stats.query_plan_text` columns with **no Postgres schema change** â€” PostgreSQL TOAST compresses the plan text transparently (pglz; lz4 a future option) and TimescaleDB chunk compression squeezes it further, so plans are cheap to keep centrally, the opposite of Lite's DuckDB/Parquet store where they were deliberately dropped. **Lite is unaffected**: it never sets the flag, so its query_stats/query_store SQL is byte-identical to before and it still captures no plans â€” pinned by collector-definition tests (flag-off emits no plan clauses and the erased placeholders leave no trace; flag-on emits the exact Dashboard-mirrored `dm_exec_text_query_plan` / `query_store_plan` shapes) plus a gated live E2E (DARLING_TEST_SQL + DARLING_TEST_PG) that toggles the flag against SQL Server into Postgres and asserts a captured plan parses as ShowPlanXML while a flag-off run stores NULL
- **Darling Viewer W2a: the top-level aggregate surfaces â€” the Overview server-cards grid and full Alert History parity, copied from Lite** ([#1262]) â€” the two all-servers surfaces at the top of the tab strip. **Overview** is a NEW first tab (before Recommendations): a `WrapPanel` of 260-px cards, one per registered server, each showing the server name + status dot, CPU (total non-idle with the SQL-only number alongside), memory, blocking and deadlock counts (last hour), and last-collection time, with Lite's per-metric colour bands (CPU â‰¥ 80 red / â‰¥ 50 amber / green, blocking/deadlock red-or-amber-when-present) and the red **Offline** overlay â€” copied from Lite's `MainWindow` Overview (`GetServerSummaryAsync` + `ServerSummaryItem`) and rewired to Postgres, reading each server's latest metrics over the same `v_*` passthrough views the other tabs use (CPU, memory, XE-blocking-with-DMV-fallback, deadlocks, `MAX(collection_time)`). Double-clicking a card opens that server's tab; the grid refreshes on its own 30-second timer (Lite's Overview cadence) and always shows every server (it ignores the sidebar selection). **The one semantic change** (#1262 headless plan): Lite's card status comes from a live per-server connection ping, which the viewer doesn't have â€” so the viewer derives status from **collection freshness** instead, banding the newest `collection_log` age (fresh â†’ Online, older than twice the fastest collector's one-minute cadence â†’ the amber Warning state, no collection or long-dead â†’ Offline) onto Lite's own card inputs, so the card view-model is otherwise a verbatim copy. **Alert History** is upgraded from the shell's server-scoped read pane to Lite's full `AlertsHistoryTab` surface: **all servers by default** with a Server column and a Server filter combo (`GetAlertHistoryAsync` gains the optional-serverId all-servers form), **Dismiss Selected / Dismiss All** (the write path â€” a set-based `UPDATE â€¦ SET dismissed = TRUE` keyed on `(alert_time, server_id, metric_name)`, dismissed rows dropping out of the default read), a modal **Alert Details** window copied from Lite (structured advice / remediation T-SQL / drill-down from `context_json`, falling back to `detail_text`) replacing the inline pane, column-filter headers, Copy Cell/Row/All + Export CSV, alert-count and last-refreshed indicators, and **Mute This Alert / Mute Similar** alongside the existing Manage Mute Rules window â€” mute lives on this tab (Erik's faithful-copy decision). **Two documented deviations from Lite:** Darling has no parquet archive tier, so the dismiss collapses from Lite's two-phase live-table-UPDATE-plus-`dismissed_archive_alerts`-sidecar into one atomic UPDATE (no sidecar, no archived-row states/banner), and the card's CPU headline always uses total non-idle CPU because the viewer has no per-app `CpuAlertMode` preference. **No Lite, Dashboard, or shared `.Ui` code changed.** Covered by ungated pins (the five summary reads' clauses + dialect + real-column checks, the freshness-status banding and card display, the all-servers/per-server alert reads and the dismiss SQL) and gated live-Postgres round-trips (the summary read against planted CPU/memory/blocking/deadlock/collection rows with the XEâ†’DMV fallback and Online-from-fresh-collection, and the dismiss round-trip: a single dismiss and a dismiss-all vanish from the default read while a second server's rows remain)
- **Darling Viewer: the Plan Viewer inner tab lights up View Plan against the stored plans â€” copied from Lite** ([#1262]) â€” the viewer now HOSTS execution plans, consuming the `query_plan_xml` / `query_plan_text` the service captures (the "Darling captures execution plans" work above). Lite's **Plan Viewer** inner tab is copied onto the W0 shell at Lite's position (between Queries and CPU â€” the inner-tab index constants renumber, CPUâ†’4 â€¦ Collection Healthâ†’12), hosting the shared `PerformanceMonitor.Ui` `PlanViewerControl` as closable sub-tabs with the empty-state / loading-overlay / +close chrome byte-for-byte from Lite's `ServerTab.Plans.cs` / `.xaml`. The one seam-change from Lite is the plan SOURCE: Lite fetches plans live from the monitored server's plan cache, but the headless viewer never connects to SQL Server, so it reads the plan text the service already stored in Postgres â€” two new keyed reads (`GetQueryStatsPlanXmlAsync` by `(server, database, query_hash)`; `GetQueryStorePlanTextAsync` by `(server, database, query_id, plan_id)`), each returning the newest non-null plan for the key (`ORDER BY collection_time DESC LIMIT 1`, read as text with no length cap â€” TOAST owns storage). **View Plan** is wired on the **Top Queries** and **Query Store** grids (a split plan-enabled context menu; right-click â†’ open the stored plan in a Plan Viewer sub-tab), and **Top Queries** also gains Lite's **Query Plan** Download column that saves the stored `.sqlplan` â€” gated on a new `HasQueryPlan` flag the grid read now carries (`bool_or(query_plan_xml IS NOT NULL)` over the group, so the multi-KB plan never rides in every row; the plan itself is fetched on demand). The shared `OpenPlanTab(planXml, label, queryText)` entry point is the hook every View-Plan surface calls (including the parallel W1f-2 Active Queries snapshots, which carry their own plan XML). Deliberately DEFERRED, each with an honest in-code note: **Top Procedures** (the `procedure_stats` collector stores only a `plan_handle`, no plan XML â€” resolving it needs a live server, which Lite has and the viewer doesn't) keeps the plan-less context menu; **Blocking / Deadlocks** View-Plan (the reports carry statement text + a `sql_handle`, not plan XML); and **Get Actual Plan** everywhere (a live re-execution). **Zero Lite/Dashboard/.Ui changes.** Pinned by `ViewerPlanHostTests`: ungated SQL / dialect / gating pins (the two keyed plan reads' columns + `IS NOT NULL` + newest-wins ordering, the `has_query_plan` presence flag threaded through `TopQueriesSql`, PG positional params) plus gated (DARLING_TEST_PG) live round-trips â€” a planted `query_stats` plan fetched back newest-first with `HasQueryPlan` true, a NULL-plan row fetching null with the gate false, and a `query_store_stats` plan round-tripped by `(query_id, plan_id)` with a NULL/absent plan_id returning null
- **Darling M3: the first Viewer shell â€” servers, collection health, wait trend** ([#1262]) â€” the Darling viewer now reads the central Postgres store directly: a server list from `servers`, a per-collector Collection Health grid from `collection_log` (latest run per collector, status-colored), and a top-8-wait-types trend chart from `wait_stats`, refreshing the selected server every 60 seconds with all loads async off the UI thread. The chart rides the shared `ChartStyle` chrome and `ChartPalette` colors, and the viewer reads the same `darling.json` the service uses (just `postgres.connectionString`), showing a copy-the-sample hint instead of crashing when it's missing. **Viewer wave 2 restructures the right side into surface tabs and adds three of them** ([#1262]): **Queries** â€” the top-50 query-stats groups over the last 24 hours (database, truncated query text with a tooltip, execution/CPU/duration/reads deltas, last execution), ranked by summed elapsed-time delta exactly like Lite's Top Queries default sort, with the latest captured text fetched per group via a LATERAL join and Lite's WAITFOR trim; **Blocking** â€” recent blocked processes with the XE reports preferred and the always-on DMV snapshot merged in as fallback through the SHARED `BlockedProcessReportMerge` on the shared `BlockedProcessAlertRow` shape (the alert path's exact dedup/re-cap semantics), each row badged with its Source (XE report vs DMV snapshot); **Recommendations** â€” the latest analysis run's findings read through the shared `PgFindingStore` contract, severity-banded with both apps' cutoffs and colored like the health statuses, with a read-only detail pane composing the story via `FactAdvice.GetForFinding` (advice prose, any derivable copy-paste T-SQL, and the persisted `remediation_action_json` pretty-printed â€” NO Apply in the viewer) and the analysis-cadence empty state. Loads stay lazy per tab (Lite's visible-only rule): selecting a server or tab loads only the active tab, and the 60-second refresh re-reads just that tab. **Viewer wave 3 adds the write-back surfaces â€” the viewer's first user-initiated writes, all straight to Postgres** ([#1262]) â€” the store is the coordination point (no IPC to the service, which honors them on its next read; the plan's Hard-Problem-3 resolution), and the viewer writes ONLY these coordination tables, never collector data. **Finding mute/unmute** in the Recommendations tab: a right-click context menu mutes or unmutes the selected finding's story pattern through the shared `PgFindingStore.MuteStoryAsync`/`UnmuteStoryAsync` (the exact statements the `mute_analysis_finding` MCP tool writes), a new "Muted" column + dimmed row flag the state, and a new `PgFindingStore.GetMutedStoriesAsync` read (carrying the `mute_id` that `GetMutedHashesSql` omits) marks the already-persisted latest-batch rows â€” muting does not retract the current batch, the engine drops the pattern on its next analysis run, exactly Lite/Dashboard mute semantics; the viewer always mutes per-selected-server, so it never takes the MCP "all servers" `server_id = 0` path â€” that shared cross-app quirk is mirrored, not fixed. A new **Alerts tab** hosts the other two surfaces: **alert history** â€” a read over `config_alert_log` for the selected server (newest first, `dismissed = FALSE`, selectable time range) mirroring Lite's `AlertHistoryRow` presentation (the metric-keyed value/threshold formatting from [#1134], email-vs-tray status, the shared `AlertMetricClassifier` critical/warning/resolved row tints + muted dimming) with a detail pane surfacing the stored detail text and the pretty-printed `context_json` [#1140] dedup fingerprint; read-only â€” no dismiss, because Lite's is a durable hide flag rather than cosmetic and the wave brief says not to invent one; and **mute-rule management** â€” a Manage Mute Rules window (Add / Edit / Toggle / Delete / Purge-Expired, a dark-themed port of Lite's `ManageMuteRulesWindow` + `MuteRuleDialog`) plus a "Create mute rule from this alert" context action, doing INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE against `config_mute_rules` with the same columns/semantics the service's `PgMuteRuleStore` reads (config_mute_rules scopes by `server_name`, NOT server_id â€” Lite's `DuckDbMuteRuleStore`/`MuteRule.Matches` shape, mirrored exactly; the `server_id = 0` quirk lives in the analysis-finding mute path, not here). Additive â€” the four existing tabs are untouched, the new dark windows share one `ViewerDarkTheme` dictionary, and every write is parameterized naive-UTC. Pinned by `ViewerWave3Tests`: ungated SQL/dialect/schema-parity and pure-display pins, plus gated (DARLING_TEST_PG) live round-trips â€” mute â†’ fresh read shows muted with its mute_id â†’ unmute; mute-rule insert/update/toggle/delete + purge-expired; and the alert-history read excluding dismissed rows (validated live 2026-07-02 against the portable PG 17.10). **Viewer wave 4 turns the Overview into a two-trend-chart glance** ([#1262]): a **CPU-utilization trend** â€” SQL Server vs other-process CPU %, the series names + `ChartPalette.SeriesColor` blue/red mirrored from Lite's `ServerTab.Charts.cs` CPU chart, averaged per collection so the ring buffer's up-to-60-samples-per-run collapse to one clean point (`AVG(integer)` CAST to double precision for the typed reader, a NULL other-process sample reading as 0 for the SQL-on-Linux case) â€” sits beside a **wait-time-by-category trend** that supersedes wave 1's per-wait-type chart: every wait type is rolled up through the shared `ChartPalette.WaitCategory` classifier (the SAME ~21-category taxonomy the plan-viewer wait list uses) and each category is drawn in its fixed `ChartPalette.WaitColor`, so a category reads as one color everywhere in the product instead of the old index-cycled per-type colors. Both charts window on the naive-UTC `collection_time` like every other Darling read (the CPU table's own `sample_time` is server-LOCAL wall clock and is deliberately not used for the axis, matching `PgFactCollector`'s CPU read), convert to local via `ViewerDataService.ToLocalTime`, and ride the shared `ChartStyle` chrome + `StyleScatter` line polish; Collection Health keeps the top half. The category roll-up (`RollUpWaitCategories`) is pure and unit-tested (per-category-and-time summing, total-descending rank with a deterministic tie-break, the top-N cap, the shared classifier), the two reads are SQL/dialect/schema-parity pinned by `ViewerTrendsTests`, and gated (DARLING_TEST_PG) live round-trips prove the CPU averaging + NULL-other-as-0 and the wait roll-up against real Postgres (validated live 2026-07-02 against the portable PG 17.10)
- **Darling Viewer W1a: the CPU and tempdb inner tabs, copied from Lite** ([#1262]) â€” the first copy-parity tab port on top of the W0 shell: `ViewerServerTab` gains a **CPU** tab and a **tempdb** tab (appended after the four W0 tabs, CPU before tempdb, matching Lite's relative order), their XAML/hover/colors copied from Lite's `ServerTab` with only the data layer rewired to Postgres. **CPU** is one full-bleed scatter chart of RAW per-sample CPU (SQL Server vs other processes) â€” a new `ViewerDataService.GetCpuUtilizationAsync` reading every ring-buffer sample from `cpu_utilization_stats` (not the Overview's average-per-collection `CpuTrendSql`), keeping Lite's chart shape exactly: `SeriesColor("SqlCpu"/"OtherCpu")`, `StyleScatter`, fixed `SetLimitsY(0, 105)`, "CPU %" label, bottom legend, and a "%"-unit hover. **tempdb** is three stacked charts like Lite â€” space usage (user/internal/version-store via `SeriesColor` + `UnallocatedTempdb`), total allocated size on its own `AutoScaleY` scale, and per-file I/O latency in the cycling `ChartPalette` palette â€” over ported reads of `v_tempdb_stats` and `v_file_io_stats` (the `SELECT *` passthrough views, mirroring Lite's view-based queries; file-I/O filtered to `database_name = 'tempdb'`). PG dialect notes carried by the reads: the `numeric(18,2)` MB columns are CAST to double precision for the typed GetDouble reader, and `total_sessions_using_tempdb` is read with GetInt64 (it's `bigint` â€” Lite's DuckDB GetInt32 would throw against Postgres). One deliberate deviation from Lite, noted in-code: Lite plots the CPU chart's raw server-LOCAL `sample_time` directly, but the viewer has no per-server offset, so `sample_time` runs through `ViewerDataService.ToLocalTime` (the naive-UTC-to-viewer-local convention every Darling chart uses) for the axis. Loads stay lazy per inner tab (Lite's visible-only rule) and the 60-second timer refreshes only the active tab; the hover helpers are torn down when the server tab closes. Deliberately NOT ported (deferred): Lite's per-chart context menu and its "Show Active Queries at This Time" drill-down (the viewer has no drill-down surfaces yet), and Lite's `Task.Run` chart-load wrapping (Npgsql is genuinely async). **Zero Lite/Dashboard changes.** Pinned by `ViewerCpuTempDbTests`: ungated SQL-const pins (the raw-not-averaged CPU shape, the MB CASTs, the bigint session count left un-cast, the tempdb file-I/O filter/grouping, PG-dialect positional params) plus gated (DARLING_TEST_PG) live round-trips â€” raw CPU samples ordered by sample_time with a NULL other-process reading as 0, the tempdb MB-as-double read with a `total_sessions` value past int.MaxValue proving GetInt64, and the file-I/O read returning tempdb files only with per-file average latency (validated live 2026-07-03 against the portable PG 17.10)
- **Darling Viewer W1c: the File I/O tab and the Blocking tab's Trends + Current Waits sub-tabs, copied from Lite** ([#1262]) â€” two more copy-parity ports on the W0 shell, XAML/hover/colors copied from Lite's `ServerTab` with only the data layer rewired to Postgres. A new **File I/O** inner tab (a nested sub-TabControl) with **File I/O Latency** â€” stacked read + write per-file latency charts with Lite's dashed queued-I/O overlay â€” and **File I/O Throughput** â€” stacked read + write MB/s charts â€” each plotting the top 10 files (by delta ops / delta bytes) in the cycling `ChartPalette` palette; ported reads of `v_file_io_stats` (`GetFileIoLatencyTrendAsync` averaging stall/op with the queued-stall COALESCE'd, `GetFileIoThroughputTrendAsync` deriving MB/s from the `LAG`-based collection interval, dropping each file's first NULL-interval row â€” Lite's `EXTRACT(EPOCH ...)` runs verbatim on PG). The **Blocking** tab gains Lite's sub-tab structure: **Trends** (lock-wait rate from `v_wait_stats` LIKE 'LCK%', blocking incidents from `v_blocked_process_reports` with the `v_dmv_blocking_snapshots` fallback, deadlocks from `v_deadlocks` bucketed on `deadlock_time`) and **Current Waits** (waiting-task duration by wait type, blocked sessions by database) precede the existing blocked-process-report grid, which is re-hosted UNCHANGED as the third "Blocked Process Reports" sub-tab. The trend charts keep Lite's spike-plot / zero-line-when-empty shapes and the fixed `ChartPalette.SeriesColor` identities (LockWaits / Blocking / Deadlocks / CurrentWaits / BlockedSessions). Two deviations from Lite, both noted in-code: the time axis runs through `ViewerDataService.ToLocalTime` instead of Lite's per-server `UtcOffsetMinutes` shift, and the two Current-Waits reads hit the **base `waiting_tasks` table directly** â€” the Darling store has no `v_waiting_tasks` passthrough view (it was left out of the V4/V5 migration view lists; the base table has existed since V1, so no migration is added this wave) â€” with the bigint-duration SUM CAST back to bigint for the typed reader (SUM of a bigint is `numeric` in Postgres). Sub-tab loads stay gated (Lite's subTabOnly rule): activating the File I/O or Blocking tab, switching a sub-tab, and the 60-second timer each load only the active sub-tab, routed through the shell's overlap-guarded refresh. Tab placement: File I/O sits between tempdb and Blocking (grouping it with the tempdb tab's per-file I/O chart) â€” Lite's own order has File I/O just before tempdb, after the not-yet-ported Memory tab; the tab is a trivial reorder if strict Lite order is preferred. Deliberately NOT ported (deferred): Lite's chart context menus and "Show ... at This Time" drill-downs, per-chart save-image, and the Blocking Deadlocks grid sub-tab (a later wave). **Zero Lite/Dashboard changes.** Pinned by `ViewerFileIoBlockingTests`: ungated SQL-const / dialect / schema-parity pins (the top-files CTEs, the queued COALESCE, the `LAG` per-second math, the XE-preferred-with-DMV-fallback UNION, the `waiting_tasks` base-table reads and the bigint SUM cast) plus gated (DARLING_TEST_PG) live round-trips for all seven reads â€” per-file latency with the queued overlay, LAG throughput dropping the first NULL interval, the DMV fallback appearing exactly when no XE rows exist, deadlock bucketing on deadlock_time, the LCK%-only lock-wait per-second rate, the bigint-SUM duration read via GetInt64, and the blocked-session count filtered to blocking rows
- **Darling Viewer W1e: the Blocking tab reaches full Lite depth â€” widened Blocked Process Reports, the Deadlocks sub-tab, and the block-chain + deadlock-graph viewers, copied from Lite** ([#1262]) â€” W1c left the "Blocked Process Reports" sub-tab as the old flat 9-column grid; W1e brings the whole Blocking tab to Lite parity. **Blocked Process Reports** becomes Lite's full ~25-column, per-column-filterable grid: the read widens to Lite's 37-column `GetRecentBlockedProcessReportsAsync` SELECT over `v_blocked_process_reports` â€” now **including `blocked_process_report_xml`** (W1c deliberately trimmed it; the XML gates the per-row **Save** button via `HasReportXml` and feeds the block-chain viewer) â€” merged with the 19-column `v_dmv_blocking_snapshots` fallback through the SHARED `BlockedProcessReportMerge` on the shared `BlockedProcessAlertRow` shape (the alert path's exact XE-preferred dedup/re-cap semantics), each row keeping its **Source badge**, the W1g `ColumnFilterButtonStyle` filter headers, and Lite's `BlockingRowStyle` long-block (`WaitTimeMs > 30000`) red tint. A new **Deadlocks** sub-tab hosts Lite's per-process grid: `GetRecentDeadlocksAsync` reads `v_deadlocks` (carrying `deadlock_graph_xml`), and a copied `DeadlockProcessDetail.ParseFromRows` walks each graph into one row per process (victim detection, regular-vs-parallel, owner/waiter modes, object names, proc-name resolution) â€” **off the UI thread** (Lite's #1193 fix; the parse is CPU-bound XML work) â€” with its own XML **Save** gated on `HasDeadlockXml`. Both sub-tabs carry a **copy of Lite's UTC-based `TimeRangeSlicerControl`** (namespace-only change; its two display strings swapped from Lite's per-server `ServerTimeHelper.FormatServerTime` to the viewer's `ViewerDataService.ToLocalTime`), fed by ported `GetBlockingSlicerDataAsync` / `GetDeadlockSlicerDataAsync` bucket reads (blocking XE-preferred with the DMV exclusion; deadlocks by hour), and dragging the slicer re-reads its grid over the selection through a `DataGridFilterManager` so active column filters survive. **View Block Chain** (right-click / double-click a blocked-process row) reuses Darling.Analysis's internal `PgBlockingPairRowQuery` (the fetch + DMV-snapshot merge, built from the shared column fragments the drill-down + fact collectors also use, so the apex + column order can't drift) via a new internal `ViewerDataService.GetBlockingPairRowsAsync`, feeds the shared `BlockingChainReconstructor`, and bridges the reconstruction to the Common `BlockingChainModel` through a viewer copy of `BlockingChainViewerProjection` â€” fetch + reconstruct + tree-build inside `Task.Run`, the finished model rendered in the shared `.Ui` `BlockingChainControl` + `GraphViewerWindow`; the reconstruction window is derived straight from the clicked row's `event_time` with NO clock conversion (the pair-row query filters on that same naive-UTC column). **View Deadlock Graph** (right-click / double-click a deadlock row) parses the row's carried XML off the UI thread through the shared `DeadlockGraphParser` into the shared `.Ui` `DeadlockGraphControl`. Copy/Export on both context menus delegate to the shared `.Ui` `DataGridExport`. Deliberately DEFERRED (noted in-code): the two **View-Plan** context items (Lite's View Blocked/Blocking Plan on BPR, View Plan on Deadlocks) â€” the viewer has no plan host yet. The one shared-assembly touch is a single `InternalsVisibleTo` line: `PerformanceMonitor.Analysis` now exposes its internals to the viewer exactly as it already does to Lite, Dashboard, and Darling.Analysis, so the viewer can call the reconstructor and own its projection copy the same per-app way the two apps do. **Zero Lite/Dashboard/.Ui changes.** Pinned by `ViewerBlockingDepthTests`: ungated SQL/dialect/schema-parity pins (the 37-column BPR read incl. the report XML, the 19-column DMV fallback without it, the pair-row read's shared fragments + SpidFilter, the deadlock read, both hourly slicer bucket shapes, every selected column present in the collector-generated DDL) plus pure `DeadlockProcessDetail.ParseFromRows` pins (a two-process graph resolving victim/owner/waiter/object/proc-name, the malformed-XML single-victim fallback, the empty-graph skip) plus gated (DARLING_TEST_PG) live round-trips â€” the widened BPR read surfacing the report XML + long-block + Source badge + the columns the old 9-column read dropped, the DMV fallback appearing with its Source when no XE rows exist, the deadlock read feeding the parser, and both slicer bucket reads (blocking XE-preferred with the DMV excluded). W1c's now-inverted "deliberately skips the report XML" pin is replaced by the include-XML pin, and `EventTimeLocal` (now the Lite-grid display string, not a `DateTime?`) has its display test updated
- **Darling Viewer W1f-1: the Queries tab becomes Lite's nested sub-tab group â€” the three big grids (Top Queries / Top Procedures / Query Store), copied from Lite** ([#1262]) â€” the flat top-50 Queries grid (wave 2's `QueryStatsGridRow` / `GetTopQueriesAsync`, 7 columns) is **ABSORBED and replaced**: the Queries inner tab is now Lite's nested `QueriesSubTabControl` holding three full grids on the W0 shell, XAML/columns/filters copied from Lite's `ServerTab` with only the data layer rewired to Postgres. **Top Queries by Duration** is Lite's full ~44-column grid â€” the absorbed top-50 grid's replacement â€” with four in-grid `ui:BarChartCell` columns (Executions / Total CPU / Total Duration / Total Reads, filled against host `BarMax*` dependency properties recomputed on every load + filter) and the **CPU-by-database** `ui:MetricBarCards` expander, over `GetTopQueriesByCpuAsync` (Lite's read: despite the name it ranks by summed `delta_elapsed_time` DESC, matching the grid's TotalElapsedMs default sort, with the latest non-null text per group via a LATERAL join and Lite's over-fetch-by-5 WAITFOR trim). **Top Procedures by Duration** is Lite's ~30-column grid over `GetTopProceduresByCpuAsync` (grouped by database/schema/object/type, `FullName` composed like Lite), and **Query Store by Duration** is Lite's ~52-column grid + the `IsForcedPlan` checkbox column over `GetQueryStoreTopQueriesAsync` (`query_store_stats` in the Darling store). Each grid carries the W1g `ColumnFilterButtonStyle` per-column filter headers (a `DataGridFilterManager<T>` per grid joins the Configuration/Blocking set), the shared copy/export context menu (`DataGridExport`), and a **copy of Lite's UTC `TimeRangeSlicerControl`** fed by ported hourly-bucket reads (`GetQueryStatsSlicerDataAsync` / `GetProcStatsSlicerDataAsync` / `GetQueryStoreSlicerDataAsync`): dragging the slicer re-reads its grid over the selection, and sorting a grid re-labels the slicer's aggregate curve (Lite's `*Grid_Sorting`). A shared **"Compare:"** combo above the sub-tabs drives the comparison overlay (Lite's `ServerTab.Comparison.cs`): picking a baseline (Yesterday / Last week / Same day last week) hides each sub-tab's normal grid and shows a collapsed comparison grid bound to the shared `.Ui` `QueryStatsComparisonItem` / `ProcedureStatsComparisonItem` (delta % + NEW/GONE badges, sorted NEW-first then duration-delta descending) over the three ported comparison reads. PG-dialect deviations from Lite's DuckDB, all noted in-code: summed `delta_*` aggregates are `CAST(... AS bigint)` for the typed `GetInt64` readers (Postgres `SUM(bigint)` returns numeric); Lite's server-local `last_execution_time` staleness filter and its `utcOffsetMinutes` param are DROPPED (the viewer has no per-server offset; the delta HAVING already excludes never-ran plans); Query Store's forced-plan flag folds through `bool_or(is_forced_plan)` because Postgres has no `MAX(boolean)`; and the comparison CTE INNER JOINs keep Lite's null-safe `IS NOT DISTINCT FROM` but the outer **FULL JOIN uses `COALESCE(key,'')` equality** because Postgres only FULL-JOINs on merge/hash-joinable conditions (the last two caught by the gated round-trips, not the string pins) â€” the base `procedure_stats` table is read directly (no `v_procedure_stats` view exists). `last_execution_time` / `creation_time` / `cached_time` / `first_execution_time` are the server's LOCAL wall clock (dm_exec_*), shown raw via `FormatServerClock` (NOT the naive-UTC-to-local conversion the `collection_time` columns get), matching the absorbed grid's convention. Deliberately DEFERRED (noted in-code, no plan host / no live-server connection string in the headless viewer): Lite's "Query Plan" download columns on the two stats grids and the View-Plan / Get-Actual-Plan context items (the now-collected `query_plan_xml` / `query_plan_text` are not read this wave), the per-row double-click history windows, and the slicer overlay-on-select â€” only the slicer's drag-to-narrow + sort-driven metric re-labeling are wired. Deferred to **W1f-2**: the Performance Trends / Active Queries / Query Heatmap sub-tabs (their slots are ABSENT). **Zero Lite/Dashboard/.Ui changes.** Pinned by `ViewerQueriesTests`: ungated SQL/dialect/schema-parity pins (the ranking clause + over-fetch + WAITFOR trim, the LATERAL latest-text shape without the plan, every summed aggregate CAST to bigint, the dropped staleness filter, `bool_or` for forced-plan, memory pagesâ†’MB, the top-100-union / FULL-JOIN-with-COALESCE comparison shape, the execution-count-weighted Query Store averages, all three hourly slicer bucket shapes, base-table names, positional-param / no-`now(` / no-`N'` / no-`@` contract) plus pure display pins (raw server-clock formatting, `FullName`, TotalDuration, the shared comparison-item badges) plus gated (DARLING_TEST_PG) live round-trips for all three reads + a comparison + a slicer â€” grouping/summing across collections, the WAITFOR trim, unit conversion (usâ†’ms, memory pagesâ†’MB), forced-plan boolean, NEW/GONE flagging across windows, and hourly bucketing (validated live 2026-07-03 against the portable PG 17.10; the round-trips caught two real DuckDBâ†’Postgres dialect bugs â€” `MAX(boolean)` and `FULL JOIN` on `IS NOT DISTINCT FROM` â€” before merge)
- **Darling Viewer W1f-2: the Queries tab is completed to Lite's full six sub-tabs â€” Performance Trends, Active Queries, Query Heatmap, copied from Lite** ([#1262]) â€” the three slots W1f-1 left ABSENT are filled, so the Queries inner tab now matches Lite's `QueriesSubTabControl` order exactly: Performance Trends and Active Queries FIRST, the three W1f-1 grids in the middle, Query Heatmap LAST. XAML/chart-bodies/columns copied from Lite's `ServerTab` with only the data layer rewired to Postgres. **Performance Trends** is Lite's 2Ã—2 of per-second trend charts (`Query`/`Proc`/`QueryStore`DurationTrendChart + `ExecutionCountTrendChart`) over four ported reads (`GetQueryDurationTrendAsync` / `GetProcedureDurationTrendAsync` / `GetQueryStoreDurationTrendAsync` / `GetExecutionCountTrendAsync`): the read SQL is byte-identical to Lite's apart from the base-table name (Lite's `v_*` views â†’ the base `query_stats` / `procedure_stats` / `query_store_stats`, matching W1f-1) because every operator Lite uses â€” `date_trunc('second', â€¦)`, the `LAG() OVER (ORDER BY collection_time)` seconds-since-previous-snapshot interval, `extract(epoch FROM â€¦)`, `CAST(â€¦ AS double precision)` â€” is native Postgres, so no dialect rewrite (unlike the heatmap); the four `Update*TrendChart` bodies are Lite's verbatim with the one shell deviation the other copied charts share (Lite's per-server `UtcOffsetMinutes` shift â†’ `ViewerDataService.ToLocalTime`), hover kept, drill-down context menu NOT ported (matching every other viewer chart). **Active Queries** is Lite's UTC `TimeRangeSlicerControl` + the ~26-column snapshot grid (`GetLatestQuerySnapshotsAsync` â€” `query_snapshots`, WAITFOR trim, ORDER BY collection_time DESC, cpu_time_ms DESC â€” and `GetActiveQuerySlicerDataAsync` hourly buckets), a new `DataGridFilterManager<ViewerQuerySnapshotRow>` joining the shared filter set, dragging the slicer re-reading the grid, sorting re-labeling the slicer curve. Two deliberate semantics changes, both noted in-code: Lite's **"Live Snapshot"** button (which queries the monitored server live) becomes **"Latest Snapshot"** and re-reads the newest STORED snapshot batch instead (`GetLatestQuerySnapshotBatchAsync` â€” the rows at `MAX(collection_time)`, the closest stored analogue of Lite's point-in-time view â€” per Erik's decision that the viewer reads only stored data), and the grid's Estimated / Actual **Query Plan** buttons (gated on `HasQueryPlan` / `HasLiveQueryPlan` over the collector's inline `query_plan` / `live_query_plan`) open the stored plan through a `partial void OpenPlanTab(string, string, string)` hook that the parallel plan-host wave implements â€” declared here as a partial-method hook so this branch compiles standalone (an unimplemented `partial void` call compiles to a no-op). **Query Heatmap** is Lite's metric combo (Duration / CPU / Logical Reads / Writes / Execution Count) + the query-count heatmap per (5-minute bin Ã— per-execution log-magnitude bucket), and carries the wave's real SQL work â€” the DuckDBâ†’Postgres rewrite of `GetQueryHeatmapAsync`: DuckDB's `time_bucket(INTERVAL '5 minutes', collection_time)` â†’ Postgres `date_bin(INTERVAL '5 minutes', collection_time, TIMESTAMP '1970-01-01 00:00:00')` (an explicit epoch origin aligns 5-minute bins to :00/:05/:10â€¦ like time_bucket's default; `date_bin` is native PG 14+, so the read stays valid on the plain-PostgreSQL store where TimescaleDB's `time_bucket` isn't guaranteed), and DuckDB's `ARG_MAX(query_hash, delta_execution_count)` (the hottest query per cell) â†’ a `ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY the cell ORDER BY delta_execution_count DESC)` top-1 window with the cell's `COUNT(*) OVER` carried alongside, so one pass yields both the count and the top row (ties resolve arbitrarily, as ARG_MAX did); the magnitude CASE, the `delta_execution_count > 0` filter, the `LEFT(query_text, 120)` preview, and the grid assembly are Lite's verbatim; render + hover bodies are Lite's (time axis via `ToLocalTime`). The heatmap's right-click **"Show Active Queries at This Time"** drill-down IS wired (the task called for it, and Active Queries is now a sibling sub-tab) â€” the ONE viewer chart with a context menu, built inline (Lite's `ContextMenuHelper` is Lite-only and can't be referenced): ScottPlot's default right-click responses are removed and a single-item WPF menu takes over, resolving the clicked 5-minute bin and switching to Active Queries filtered to a Â±window around it (a `_suppressActiveQueriesAutoRefresh` guard mirrors Lite so the tab switch doesn't clobber the filtered read); Lite's save/export menu items are dropped (the viewer never ported chart save/export). **Zero Lite/Dashboard/.Ui changes.** Pinned by `ViewerQueriesRestTests`: ungated SQL/dialect pins for the four trend reads (the LAG per-second-rate math, base-table names, positional params) and â€” the load-bearing set â€” the heatmap rewrite for EVERY metric (`time_bucket` gone / `date_bin` with epoch origin present, `ARG_MAX` gone / `ROW_NUMBER â€¦ rn = 1` + `COUNT(*) OVER` present, each metric's per-execution expression, the magnitude CASE), plus the snapshot / latest-batch / slicer reads (WAITFOR trim, `MAX(collection_time)` batch, hourly buckets), plus pure-display pins (`HasQueryPlan`/`HasLiveQueryPlan` gating, naive-UTC `CollectionTimeLocal`), plus gated (DARLING_TEST_PG) live round-trips â€” the duration trend's per-second rate across two collections with the first row's NULL interval reading 0, the heatmap binning + magnitude bucketing + top-query-by-executions (the ARG_MAX replacement) + the delta-exec-0 exclusion, the snapshot WAITFOR trim + newest-then-CPU ordering + latest-batch, and the hourly active-query slicer buckets (the gated round-trips run on a scratch PG on ports 55600â€“55699; no PG runtime was available in this environment, so they were left skip-verified rather than executed)
- **Darling Viewer W1d: the Overview inner tab becomes Lite's correlated timeline lanes, copied** ([#1262]) â€” the interim two-chart Overview (wave 4's averaged-CPU-trend + wait-by-category glance) is REPLACED by a COPY of Lite's real Overview control (`CorrelatedTimelineLanesControl`): five stacked, X-axis-synced lanes â€” CPU % (SQL vs SQL+other Total), total wait ms/sec, blocking + deadlocking bars, buffer pool MB, and file-I/O latency â€” each with a Â±2Ïƒ baseline band + anomaly-dot markers and all sharing one correlated crosshair (the shared `PerformanceMonitor.Ui` `CorrelatedCrosshairManager`, internal-visible to the viewer since W0). The render bodies (`UpdateCpuLane` / `UpdateBlockingLane` / `UpdateLane` / `ShowEmpty` / `SyncXAxes` / the theme helpers) are Lite's byte-for-byte; only the data layer is rewired. Six feeds run concurrently (genuinely async, no `Task.Run` wrap): the CPU lane REUSES W1a's raw-sample `GetCpuUtilizationAsync` (summing SQL + other-process for the Total series â€” Lite's `CpuUtilizationData.TotalCpu`), the blocking/deadlock/file-I/O lanes REUSE W1c's `GetBlockingTrendAsync` / `GetDeadlockTrendAsync` / `GetFileIoLatencyTrendAsync`, and two reads are genuinely NEW (`ViewerDataService.OverviewLanes.cs`): the single-line **total wait trend** (Lite's `GetTotalWaitTrendAsync` â€” `SUM(delta_wait_time_ms)` across all types per collection, divided by the `LAG`-derived interval for a per-second rate; Lite's per-user `IgnoredWaitTypes` exclusion DROPPED, matching W1b) and the **memory trend** (Lite's `GetMemoryTrendAsync` â€” the four `numeric(18,2)` MB metrics CAST to double, the buffer-pool lane using `BufferPoolMb`; the full four-metric row carried for parity with a future Memory tab, like the wait picker's signal-wait field). The lanes' baseline bands + anomaly dots wire the shared `PgBaselineProvider` through a new `GetBaselineForLaneAsync` (Lite's mirror over the lazily-built provider) for the four chart-unit metrics (`Cpu` / `WaitMsPerSec` / `IoLatency` / `BlockingPerMinute`), returning `BaselineBucket.Empty` on no history so the band simply doesn't render. Two deviations from Lite, both noted in-code: the time axis runs through `ViewerDataService.ToLocalTime` instead of Lite's per-server `ServerTimeHelper.UtcOffsetMinutes` shift â€” ALL lanes plot `collection_time` via `ToLocalTime`, and the CPU lane plots the server-LOCAL `sample_time` also via `ToLocalTime` (the same deviation the W1a CPU tab documents) â€” and `AppLogger` becomes `Debug.WriteLine`. Deliberately DROPPED for v1 (noted): Lite's comparison-range ghost-line overlay (the whole `comparisonRange` block + `AddGhostLine` + `ComparisonLabel`). Deliberately KEPT but UNWIRED: the lanes' "Show Active Queries at This Time" right-click drill-down event â€” the viewer has no Active Queries surface yet (deferred). Wave 4's now-superseded interim reads (`CpuTrendSql` / `WaitCategoryTrendSql` / `GetCpuTrendAsync` / `GetWaitCategoryTrendAsync` / `RollUpWaitCategories`, all Overview-only in `ViewerDataService.Trends.cs`) and the two interim render methods are REMOVED, and their `ViewerTrendsTests` with them â€” the lanes plot raw CPU + a single total-wait line rather than an averaged CPU trend + per-category roll-up, so those reads have no remaining consumer; the shared `ChartPalette.WaitCategory` classifier the roll-up exercised is still covered by the plan-viewer wait list. **Zero Lite/Dashboard/.Ui changes.** Pinned by `ViewerOverviewLanesTests`: ungated SQL/dialect/schema-parity pins for the two new reads (the SUM-per-collection + LAG per-second math with the dropped `NOT IN` exclusion, the four MB CASTs, the columns present in the collector-generated tables, PG positional params) plus gated (DARLING_TEST_PG) live round-trips â€” the total-wait per-second rate summing across types with the first collection's NULL interval reading 0, the four-MB memory read preserving `numeric(18,2)` cents as doubles, and the baseline lane read returning Empty on no history (the baseline computation itself stays covered by the Analysis suite)
- **Darling Viewer W1i: the Daily Summary inner tab + Collection Health upgraded to Lite's 3-sub-tab shape â€” copied from Lite** ([#1262]) â€” the two remaining Collection-family tabs Lite carries at the end of `ServerTab`, reproduced on the W0 shell in Lite's exact order (Daily Summary between Configuration and Collection Health), reads rewired to Postgres, nothing reinvented; the inner-tab index constants renumber so Collection Health shifts to 11. **Daily Summary** is Lite's date-picker-driven one-row roll-up (`GetDailySummaryAsync` from `LocalDataService.DailySummary.cs`): a Today button + explicit UTC date picker + Refresh drive a single scalar-subquery SELECT that rolls the selected day up across every source view â€” total wait seconds and the top wait type (`v_wait_stats`, positive deltas only), the distinct query count (`v_query_stats`), deadlock count (`v_deadlocks`), blocking-event count (`v_blocked_process_reports` with the `NULLIF`â†’`v_dmv_blocking_snapshots` fallback), the high-CPU-sample count (`v_cpu_utilization_stats` on the **total host CPU = SQL + other-process â‰¥ 80** rule, with the Linux NULL-other-process â†’ 0 fallback, matching the alert engine / Overview headline / Dashboard `report.daily_summary`), and the `ERROR` collector count (`v_collection_log`) â€” with the NORMAL / DEADLOCKS / CPU_CRITICAL / BLOCKING health band computed in C# exactly like Lite. The SQL is byte-portable between DuckDB and Postgres (positional `$1/$2/$3` referenced across every subquery, `COALESCE`/`NULLIF`, no dialect functions), so only the parameter binding differs: the half-open `[dayStart, dayEnd)` boundaries bind with `DateTimeKind.Unspecified` per the naive-UTC store convention; changing the date only updates the indicator (the load fires on Today/Refresh), matching Lite. **Collection Health** REPLACES the shell's placeholder (a single latest-run-per-collector grid + a simple `CollectorHealthRow` record) with Lite's rich 3-sub-tab surface: **Health Summary** â€” Lite's 7-day per-collector aggregate (`GetCollectionHealthAsync`) with the rich `CollectorHealthRow` class ported verbatim, its `HealthStatus` banding intact (NEVER_RUN / NO_PERMISSIONS / FAILING / STALE / WARNING / HEALTHY, the on-load-collector staleness exemption, SKIPPED-counts-as-healthy [#1248], and `FailureRatePercent`), the W1g `ColumnFilterButtonStyle` filter headers, and **double-click a collector to open its full run history** in a copied `CollectionLogWindow` drill (`GetCollectionLogByCollectorAsync`, repointed to Postgres, using the shared `.Ui` `DataGridExport`); **Collection Log** â€” Lite's recent run log (`GetRecentCollectionLogAsync`) with per-run SQL and store-write timings and row counts, where the store's `duckdb_duration_ms` column (which in the Darling store records the **Postgres write phase**) is surfaced as the **"Store (ms)"** column; **Duration Trends** â€” Lite's per-collector success-duration scatter (`UpdateCollectorDurationChart`), cycling the shared `ChartPalette` with `ChartStyle.StyleScatter` line polish. The **name-collision reconciliation**: the rich `CollectorHealthRow` class (7-day aggregate, no `CollectionTime`/`CollectionTimeLocal`) replaces the shell's placeholder record and its DISTINCT-ON read + `CollectionHealthSql` const are removed; `StatusToBrushConverter` stays wired ONLY to the Recommendations severity bands (Lite doesn't color the health status â€” the Health Summary status is plain text â€” so the now-dead converter declaration is dropped from `ViewerServerTab.xaml` and the converter's doc + the `SeverityBand` doc are de-staled), and the two new `DataGridFilterManager`s (`CollectorHealthRow` + `CollectionLogRow`) join the shared filter set. Both reads follow the store's naive-UTC convention (`ToLocalTime`, Npgsql `Unspecified`â†’UTC), the aggregate SUM/COUNT/AVG results read type-agnostically (Postgres `bigint`/`numeric` where DuckDB had HUGEINT/DECIMAL), and the tab loads lazily with the 60-second timer refreshing only the active inner tab. Deliberate deviations from Lite, all noted in-code: Lite's **"Open Log File"** button is dropped (it opens Lite's local log file, which the viewer has no equivalent of), the **StatusToBrush** color on the health status is dropped (Lite plain text), and Lite's per-chart context menu + `Task.Run` chart-load wrapping are not ported (the viewer's established convention â€” no drill-down surfaces, Npgsql genuinely async); the DatePicker + its calendar theme declaratively from `DarkTheme.xaml`, so Lite's imperative `CalendarOpened` re-theming handler is not needed (the viewer is fixed-Dark). **Zero Lite/Dashboard/.Ui changes.** Pinned by `ViewerDailyHealthTests`: ungated SQL / dialect / schema-parity pins (the daily aggregate's per-source subqueries + top-wait ranking + XEâ†’DMV blocking fallback + total-CPU â‰¥ 80 rule + half-open day window, the 7-day health aggregate's banding buckets + SKIPPED-as-healthy last-success, both log reads incl. the `sql_duration_ms`/`duckdb_duration_ms` columns, every read's positional-param / no-`now(` / no-`N'` / no-`@` contract, the source columns present in the collector-generated DDL, the collection_log columns in the migration, and the `v_*` passthrough views present in the migrations), pure-helper pins (the full `HealthStatus` banding matrix incl. the on-load exemption + high-failure WARNING, `FailureRatePercent`, and the wait/duration/time formatting), and gated (DARLING_TEST_PG) live round-trips â€” the daily aggregate rolling up every source for the selected day (top wait ranking, distinct-query count, the â‰¥ 80 CPU rule, DEADLOCKS banding, out-of-window exclusion), the blocking-events DMV fallback when no XE rows exist, the 7-day aggregate banding HEALTHY / SKIPPED-as-HEALTHY / FAILING per collector, and the per-collector log filtering to one collector newest-first with the store-phase duration
- **Darling Viewer W1h: the Perfmon and Running Jobs inner tabs â€” copied from Lite** ([#1262]) â€” the two tabs Lite carries between Blocking and Configuration, reproduced on the W0 shell in that exact position (Perfmon first, then Running Jobs, matching Lite's tab order) with reads rewired to Postgres and nothing reinvented; the inner-tab index constants renumber so Configuration/Collection Health shift down two. **Perfmon** is Lite's 220px counter picker beside a full-bleed trend chart: the pack ComboBox is sourced from the SHARED `PerformanceMonitor.Ui.PerfmonPacks` (the same packs Lite and Dashboard use â€” General Throughput, Memory Pressure, CPU / Compilation, I/O Pressure, TempDB Pressure, Lock / Blocking), and every Lite behavior is preserved verbatim â€” the General-Throughput default selection, the 12-counter cap on pack fills and "Select All", checked-to-top ordering, the search filter, "Clear All" clearing only the filtered-visible set, the `_isUpdatingPerfmonSelection` reentrancy guard, and the generation-guarded batched chart update plotting each selected counter's `DeltaValue` (up to 12 series) in the cycling `ChartPalette` colors + `ChartStyle` line polish. The two reads (`GetDistinctPerfmonCountersAsync` and the batched `GetPerfmonTrendsByCountersAsync`) port from Lite's `LocalDataService.Perfmon.cs` to the `v_perfmon_stats` passthrough view, Lite's dynamic `IN ($4, $5, ...)` list unchanged; the one PG-dialect deviation, noted in-code, is `CAST(SUM(cntr_value) AS bigint)` / `CAST(SUM(delta_cntr_value) AS bigint)` because Postgres' `SUM(bigint)` returns numeric while the typed `GetInt64` reader (and DuckDB) want an integral type. **Running Jobs** is the latest-snapshot grid of currently-running SQL Agent jobs â€” the `GetRunningJobsAsync` `collection_time = MAX(...)` self-subquery ported verbatim to `v_running_jobs`, ordered by current duration descending, 8 columns with the `IsRunningLong` past-p95 row highlight and the W1g `ColumnFilterButtonStyle` filter headers (a new `DataGridFilterManager<RunningJobRow>` joins the Configuration set); the `RunningJobRow` class ports as-is (its duration/percent display columns are pure C#, and `StartTimeLocal`'s `ToLocalTime()` on the store's naive-UTC `start_time` is identical to the viewer's `ToLocalTime` convention). One deliberate deviation from Lite: Lite gates its "requires msdb access" banner on a live per-connection probe the viewer lacks, so the viewer derives it from the store â€” the banner shows when the `running_jobs` collector's most recent `collection_log` status is PERMISSIONS or ERROR (the store-side stand-in for a login that can't read msdb), a small `GetLatestRunningJobsCollectorStatusAsync` read. Both tabs keep the fixed 24-hour window (no custom-range picker), convert timestamps via `ViewerDataService.ToLocalTime`, load lazily per inner tab (Lite's visible-only rule) with the 60-second timer refreshing only the active tab, and tear down the perfmon hover through the tab's single Dispose path. Deliberately NOT ported: Lite's per-chart context menu + "Show Active Queries at This Time" drill-down (no viewer drill-down surfaces yet) and its `Task.Run` chart-load wrapping (Npgsql is genuinely async). **Zero Lite/Dashboard changes.** Pinned by `ViewerPerfmonRunningJobsTests`: ungated SQL / dialect / schema-parity pins (both perfmon reads incl. the `CAST(SUM ...)` and the dynamic IN list from `$4`, the running-jobs latest-snapshot shape, the msdb-status read's `collector_name = 'running_jobs'` / `ORDER BY collection_time DESC LIMIT 1`, every selected column present in the collector-generated table DDL, and the positional-param / no-`now(` / no-`N'` / no-`@` contract), picker-logic pins (the General-Throughput default set, the 12-counter cap, "All Counters" â†’ defaults, clear-only-filtered, and the msdb-banner derivation), and gated (DARLING_TEST_PG) live round-trips that plant perfmon rows across two collection_times and two instances (proving the per-counter SUM-across-instances, the IN filter, and delta/value plotting), plant an older + latest running-jobs snapshot (proving only-latest, duration-descending), and plant SUCCESS-then-PERMISSIONS collection_log rows (proving the newest-status msdb-banner derivation with the collector-name filter)
- **Darling Viewer W1g: the Configuration inner tab â€” Lite's four read-only snapshot grids, copied** ([#1262]) â€” the Configuration tab from Lite's `ServerTab` reproduced on the W0 shell, placed between Blocking and Collection Health (Lite's relative order; the Perfmon and Running Jobs tabs Lite carries between Blocking and Configuration arrive in later waves). It's a nested tab strip of four read-only, column-filterable grids â€” **Server Configuration** (`sys.configurations`: Setting / Configured / In Use / Dynamic / Advanced), **Database Configuration** (28 columns of `sys.databases`), **Scoped Configuration** (Database / Setting / Value / Value for Secondary), and **Trace Flags** (Trace Flag / Status / Global / Session) â€” each a **latest-snapshot** read that picks the newest capture in SQL via `capture_time = (SELECT MAX(capture_time) ...)` over the config passthrough views (`v_server_config` / `v_database_config` / `v_database_scoped_config` / `v_trace_flags`, the V4/V5 `SELECT *` twins of Lite's DuckDB views). The four reads port byte-for-byte from Lite's `LocalDataService.Config.cs` to Postgres (`ViewerDataService.Config.cs`): the SELECT column lists â€” and the database-config reader's **incrementing-ordinal mapping** â€” stay identical to Lite so the 28-column grid binds the same properties, and the four row classes port as-is (their `bool â†’ "Yes"/"No"` display projections are pure C#, so the copied XAML column bindings resolve unchanged). The grids are the **first filtered grids in the viewer**: each column header carries Lite's `ColumnFilterButtonStyle` filter button, wired through a viewer copy of Lite's `ServerTab.Filters.cs` plumbing (the `FilterButton_Click` visual-tree walk, a shared `ColumnFilterPopup`, and a `DataGridFilterManager<T>` per grid) â€” **copy-don't-promote**, so Lite/Dashboard stay untouched. On tab load the four reads fire concurrently (`Task.WhenAll`; genuinely async, no `Task.Run` wrap) and each result feeds its manager's `UpdateData`, so active column filters survive the 60-second refresh. PG dialect notes carried by the reads: they parameterize on `server_id` ($1) alone â€” no windowing $2, since the snapshot is chosen in SQL â€” and need no naive-UTC timestamp param; the grids keep the shell's `DarkGrid` chrome plus `NumericCell` right-alignment on the three numeric columns (Compat Level, Target Recovery, Trace Flag). **Zero Lite/Dashboard changes.** Pinned by `ViewerConfigurationTests`: ungated SQL/dialect/schema-parity pins (the whitespace-normalized 28-column contiguous SELECT order, the `MAX(capture_time)` latest-snapshot shape on each view, every selected column present in the collector-generated table DDL, and the server_id-only / no-$2 / no-`N'`/no-`@` dialect contract) plus gated (DARLING_TEST_PG) live round-trips that plant an older and a newer capture per read and assert the newest snapshot wins in the read's ORDER BY â€” the database-config case sets all 28 payload columns to distinct alternating values so an off-by-one in the ordinal reader would flip an asserted value
- **Darling Viewer W0: copy-parity foundations + navigation inverted to Lite's shape** ([#1262]) â€” the groundwork so the viewer can become a COPY of Lite's UI (same layout/interactions, reads rewired to Postgres) rather than a reinvention. The shell now mirrors Lite's navigation: a left sidebar server list plus ONE top tab strip holding the fixed aggregate tabs (Recommendations, Alerts â€” still server-scoped via the sidebar selection for now) alongside dynamically-added, closable per-server tabs deduped by server id â€” double-clicking a server opens (or focuses) its tab, single-clicking drives the aggregate tabs, an empty-state panel shows until the store has a server, and the 60-second timer refreshes only the visible tab (an aggregate tab for the selected server, or the visible server tab's active inner tab â€” Lite's visible-only rule). Each per-server tab hosts a new `ViewerServerTab` control whose inner tabs are the per-server surfaces relocated out of the old flat five-tab layout â€” Overview (the CPU + wait-category trend charts), Queries, Blocking, and Collection Health â€” with no change to their data or rendering. The foundations the later tab-ports build on: the shared theme dictionary is merged app-wide (`App.xaml` + `ThemeManager`, Dark fixed for v1, no picker) so copied Lite XAML resolves its `{DynamicResource}` theme keys while the viewer's existing hardcoded-hex windows and the two write-back dialogs (which keep `ViewerDarkTheme.xaml`) render exactly as before; `ColumnFilterPopup` is **copied** from Lite into the viewer (`PerformanceMonitor.Darling.Viewer`, byte-identical logic, its vestigial `Models` using dropped) â€” **copy-don't-promote**: the viewer owns its own copy and Lite/Dashboard stay untouched (zero diff), the program standard going forward per Erik's copy-parity directive (port Lite to Darling, don't refactor across the shipped apps); a chart-helper bridge (`ViewerServerTab.ChartHelpers.cs` â€” Lite's `ClearChart`/`ShowChartLegend`/theme forwarders + legend-panel tracking, copied verbatim) so later ported Render\* bodies stay byte-identical; and `PerformanceMonitor.Ui` + `PerformanceMonitor.Darling.Analysis` expose their internals to the viewer for the coming ports. The per-server-tab dedupe/close bookkeeping is a WPF-free `OpenServerTabRegistry` pinned by `ViewerServerTabRegistryTests`. `TimeRangeSlicerControl` is deliberately NOT touched this wave â€” it will be **copied** into the viewer (Lite's UTC-based version, which fits the viewer's naive-UTC store + `ToLocalTime` display as-is) when the first wave that needs it lands (the Blocking/Queries drilldown ports); the cross-app unification of Lite's and Dashboard's diverged UTC-vs-server-local slicers stays an optional future cleanup with its own risk budget, never a program dependency, and no W0 surface consumes it
- **Darling Viewer W1b: the Wait Stats inner tab â€” Lite's wait-type picker + trend, copied** ([#1262]) â€” the first copy-parity tab port on the W0 shell: Lite's Wait Stats tab (`ServerTab.xaml` + the `ServerTab.Pickers.cs` wait picker) is reproduced in the viewer's `ViewerServerTab`, placed right after Overview, reads rewired to Postgres and nothing reinvented. The 220px picker keeps every Lite behavior â€” the poison / usual-suspect / `PAGELATCH_`-prefix default selection with its 30-type cap, the search box, checked-to-top ordering, Top Waits / Clear All (Clear All clears only the filtered-visible set), the `_isUpdatingWaitTypeSelection` reentrancy guard, and the generation-guarded batched chart update with its 20-series cap and Wait Time (ms/sec) â†” Avg Wait Time (ms/wait) metric swap â€” while the two DuckDB reads (`GetDistinctWaitTypesAsync` and the batched `GetWaitStatsTrendsByTypesAsync`) run VERBATIM on the `v_wait_stats` passthrough view: Lite's per-type `LAG` per-second math and the dynamic `IN ($4, $5, ...)` list unchanged, only the parameter plumbing swapped to Npgsql (naive-UTC params), `Task.Run` dropped for real async, and timestamps shown via `ViewerDataService.ToLocalTime`. Series ride the shared `ChartPalette` cycling colors + `ChartStyle` line polish and the `ChartHoverHelper` tooltip / click-to-isolate. Two Lite pieces are deliberately DROPPED â€” the per-user `IgnoredWaitTypes` exclusion clause (no viewer equivalent) and the live-SQL "Show Queries With This Wait" drill-down + chart context menu (the viewer is store-only; deferred) â€” and the custom-date-range branch collapses to the viewer's fixed 24-hour window. Pinned by `ViewerWaitStatsTests`: ungated SQL / dialect / schema-parity pins (both reads, the dropped ignored-wait clause, the dynamic IN list starting at `$4`), picker-logic pins (`GetDefaultWaitTypes` composition + fill / 30-cap, clear-only-filtered), and gated (DARLING_TEST_PG) live round-trips proving the distinct-type ranking and the per-second + avg-per-wait trend math by type. Lite and Dashboard are untouched
- **Lite and Dashboard: the six alert-context builders move into the new shared `PerformanceMonitor.Alerting` library** ([#1262]) â€” Phase-5 slice A0+A of the alert-engine extraction. The six pure builders that render alert detail (poison wait, long-running query, volume free space, tempdb space, anomalous job, failed job) plus their pure helpers (`ContextToDetailText`, `TruncateText`, `GetBreachedVolumes`, `FormatLowDiskThreshold`) were line-identical private copies in both apps' `MainWindow.AlertEngine.cs`; both apps now call one shared `AlertContextBuilders`, identity-pinned by `AlertContextBuildersTests` in BOTH test suites so the rendered detail (and the [#1140] dedup fingerprints) can never drift again. ONE documented reconciliation: Lite's long-running-query alerts gain the ("Program", ...) detail item the Dashboard already rendered (Lite's LRQ read now populates `ProgramName` from the collected `program_name`). The six alert row types (`PoisonWaitDelta`, `LongRunningQueryInfo`, `VolumeFreeSpaceInfo`, `TempDbSpaceInfo`, `AnomalousJobInfo`, `FailedJobInfo`) become one canonical copy in the library (A0), aliased per-app so no call site changed, and the library declares the three engine seams the headless Darling alert engine builds on next â€” `IAlertEngineSettings` (the engine threshold surface, deliberately separate from Notifications' delivery-only `IAlertSettings`), `IAlertStateStore` (restart-surviving edge-trigger/failed-job watermarks, modeled on Lite's DuckDB store), and `IAlertDeliverer` (the record+send seam that keeps each app's history-row semantics app-side). The async blocking/deadlock builders and all engine loop/state/suppression logic intentionally stay app-side for later slices.
